Bifurcations for Hamiltonian systems 0 0 footnotetext: 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification . Primary 37J20, 34C23. Key words and phrases. Bifurcation, Hamiltonian system, dual variational principle, Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction.
Guangcun Lu

Abstract




Abstract
Our assumptions and results include the following special versions: For a topological space
\Lambda
and
H\in C(\Lambda\times S^{1}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n})
,
S^{1}=\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
,
suppose that each
H(\lambda,t,\cdot)
is
C^{2}
and all its partial derivatives depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,z)
and that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
there exists
1
-periodic solution
v_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n}

of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))
such that

\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto v_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}

and
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}

are also continuous.
Let
i(\gamma_{\lambda})
be the Maslov-type index of the fundamental matrix solution
\gamma_{\lambda}

of
\dot{Z}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))Z(t)
,
which is equal to the Conley-Zehnder index
\mu_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{\lambda})
if

\nu(\gamma_{\lambda}):=\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(1)-I_{2n})
is zero. Then

(i)

For some
\mu\in\Lambda
,
if there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu

and
1
-periodic solutions of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,v(t))
,

\bar{v}_{k}\neq v_{\lambda_{k}}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}

in
W^{1,2}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
, then
\nu(\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(ii)

If
\Lambda
is path-connected and there exist two distinct points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that

[i(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),i(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+\nu(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[i(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),i(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+\nu(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
\nu(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
,
then there exists
\mu\in\Lambda
for which the conditions in (i) hold and
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}

in
C^{1}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
. If
\Lambda
is first countable, and for some
\mu\in\Lambda

there exist
\lambda^{+}\neq\lambda^{-}
in any deleted neighborhood of
\mu
satisfying just conditions
then the conditions in (i) hold and
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

(iii)

Suppose that
\Lambda=(-\epsilon,\epsilon)
,
\mu=0
and
\gamma_{\lambda}
is as above. If

\nu(\gamma_{\lambda})=0
for each
\lambda\neq 0
, and
\nu(\gamma_{0})\neq 0
, and

i(\gamma_{\lambda})
takes, respectively, values
i(\gamma_{0})
and
i(\gamma_{0})+\nu(\gamma_{0})

as
\lambda\in(-\epsilon,\epsilon)
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
0
,
then

either

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,v(t))
has a sequence of solutions,
v_{\mu}^{k}\neq v_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
v_{\mu}^{k}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
;

or

for every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
\bar{v}_{\lambda}\neq v_{\lambda}
of

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))

such that
\bar{v}_{\lambda}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
;

or

for a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
,
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))

has at least two distinct solutions
v_{\lambda}^{1}\neq v_{\lambda}
and
v_{\lambda}^{2}\neq v_{\lambda}
in
\mathcal{W}
,
which can also be required to have different Hamiltonian actions
provided that
\nu(\gamma_{0})>1
and
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

(iv)

Under the assumptions of (iii), if
H
is independent of
t
and that each
v_{\lambda}
is constant (therefore

\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\exp(tJ\nabla_{z}^{2}H({\lambda},v_{\lambda}))
) and

{\rm Ker}(\nabla_{z}^{2}H({0},v_{0}))=\{0\}
,
then either
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(0,v(t))

has a sequence of
S^{1}
-distinct solutions,
\bar{v}_{k}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{0}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
v_{0}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};\mathbb{R}^{2n})
, or
there exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
0
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu(\gamma_{0})/2
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{0\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{0\}
),

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,v(t))
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
S^{1}
-distinct
solutions,
v_{\lambda}^{i}\notin S^{1}\cdot v_{\lambda}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
which converge to
v_{0}
in
C^{1}(S^{1};\mathbb{R}^{2n})
.

(v)

If
H
in (i)-(iii) is independent of
t
and all
v_{\lambda}
are equal to a nonconstant
v_{\mu}
,
corresponding bifurcation results about
S^{1}
-distinct solutions with (i)-(iii) are given.

(vi)

If
H
in (i)-(iii) also satisfies
H(\lambda,-t,(-p,q))=H(\lambda,t,(p,q))
for
all
(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}
and
(p,q)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
corresponding results about brake orbits of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))
with (i)-(iii) are given.

In addition, similar results are also proved for bifurcations for Hamiltonian paths connecting affine Lagrangian subspaces.

1
Introduction and main results

This work continues our program on variational bifurcations beginning at
[
50
,
51
]
.
The current manuscript studies bifurcations of the following Hamiltonian boundary value problem

\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H({\lambda,t},u(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]\quad\hbox{and}\quad(u(0),u(\tau))\in{\bf N}

(1.1)

with respect to a continuous family
\{u_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
of solutions of this problem parameterized by a topological space
\Lambda
,
where
{\bf N}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is a submanifold,
J
is given by (
1.2
)
and
H:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
is as in Assumption
1.1
. That is, we answer the following questions:

\bullet

Under what conditions there exists a point
\mu\in\Lambda
such that every neighborhood of

(\mu,u_{\mu})
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
contains a point
(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\notin\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

satisfying (
1.1
)?

\bullet

What are the necessary (resp. sufficient) condition for a point
(\mu,u_{\mu})
to satisfy the above properties ?

\bullet

How is the solutions of (
1.1
) distributed near the point
(\mu,u_{\mu})
as above ?

There is a vast amount of literature concerning bifurcations for periodic solutions of Hamiltonian systems,
i.e., the case that
{\bf N}
is the diagonal in
\mathbb{R}^{2n}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
and
H:\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

is
\tau
-periodic in the
\mathbb{R}
-variable, see, e.g.

[
5
,
7
,
22
,
24
,
26
,
30
,
59
,
61
,
62
,
63
,
68
,
69
]
and references therein.
Most of them considered Hamiltonians affinely depending on a real parameter or invariant for the action of a Lie group
on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, and used either
Liapunov-Schmidt reductions (
[
5
,
7
,
61
,
62
,
68
]
) or spectral flow methods (
[
22
,
30
,
62
,
63
,
69
]
)
or the Poincaré map (
[
59
,
26
]
).

In this work, using the abstract bifurcation theory recently developed by author in
[
50
,
52
]
and Appendix
C

we can prove many new bifurcation results for solutions of four types of non-autonomous Hamiltonian boundary value problems
nonlinearly depending on parameters. Our methods are also used to derive some bifurcation results starting at
a nonequilibrium (i.e., nonconstant) periodic solution of autonomous Hamiltonian systems.
Though some necessary or sufficient conditions for
bifurcations of these Hamiltonian problems may also be derived from previous abstract theories,

the most interesting and important alternative results in this paper
can only be proved with our
generalizations in
[
50
,
52
]
and Appendix
C
for the famous
Rabinowitz’s alternative bifurcation theorem
[
60
]
.
Before precisely stating these we begin with the following.

Notation and conventions
. Let
S_{\tau}:={\mathbb{R}}/\tau{\mathbb{Z}}
for
\tau>0
.
All vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
will be understand as column vectors. The transpose of a matrix
M\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times m}

is denoted by
M^{T}
.
{\rm Ker}(M)=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\,|\,Mx=0\}
.
We denote

(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathbb{R}^{m}}
by the standard Euclidean inner product in
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}

and by
|\cdot|
the corresponding norm.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{m})
be the set of all real symmetric
matrixes of order
m
, and let
{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
be the symplectic
group of real symplectic matrixes of order
2n
, i.e.,
{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})=\{M\in{\rm GL}(2n,\mathbb{R})\,|\,M^{T}JM=J_{n}\}
,
where

J_{n}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-I_{n}\\
I_{n}&0\\
\end{array}\right)

(1.2)

with the identity matrix
I_{n}
of order
n
, which gives the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,

(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n},p_{1},\cdots,p_{n})^{T}\mapsto J(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n},p_{1},\cdots,p_{n})^{T}=(-p_{1},\cdots,-p_{n},q_{1},\cdots,q_{n})^{T}.

For a map
f
from
X
to
Y
,

Df(x)
(resp.
df(x)
or
f^{\prime}(x)
) denotes the Gâteaux (resp. Fréchet) derivative of
f
at
x\in X
,
which is an element in
\mathscr{L}(X,Y)
. Of course, we also use
f^{\prime}(x)
to denote
Df(x)

without occurring of confusions. When
Y=\mathbb{R}
,
f^{\prime}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(X,\mathbb{R})=X^{\ast}
,
and if
X=H
we call the Riesz representation of
f^{\prime}(x)
in
H

gradient of
f
at
x
, denoted by
\nabla f(x)
. The Fréchet (or Gâteaux) derivative of
\nabla f
at
x\in H

is denoted by
f^{\prime\prime}(x)
or
\nabla^{2}f(x)
, which is an element in
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
.
(Precisely,
f^{\prime\prime}(x)=(f^{\prime})^{\prime}(x)\in\mathscr{L}(H;\mathscr{L}(H;\mathbb{R}))
is a symmetric bilinear form on
H
,
and is identified with
D(\nabla f)(x)
after
\mathscr{L}(H,\mathbb{R})=H^{\ast}
is identified with
H
via the Riesz representation theorem.)
For
\varepsilon>0
and a point
x
in a Banach space
X
we write
B_{X}(x,\varepsilon)=\{y\in X\,|\,\|x-y\|<\varepsilon
.

We now turn to a detailed description of our main results in four subsections.
Section 1.1 lists bifurcation results for solutions of two types of non-autonomous Hamiltonian boundary value problems.
Results in the second type immediately follows from that of the first type.
Section 1.2 is to concern with bifurcations for generalized periodic solutions of autonomous Hamiltonian systems.
In Section 1.3 we state bifurcations for brake orbits of periodic Hamiltonian systems.
Section 1.4 gives bifurcations for Hamiltonian paths connecting affine Lagrangian subspaces.

1.1.
Bifurcations for solutions of two types of non-autonomous Hamiltonian systems
.
We begin with the following two closely related assumptions.

Assumption 1.1
.

For a real
\tau>0
,
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
and a topological space
\Lambda
,
let
H:\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
each
H(\lambda,t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]
, is
C^{2}
,
and all possible partial derivatives of
H
depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
. Denote by
\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,\cdot)
the euclidian gradient of

H(\lambda,t,\cdot)
with respect to the
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
-variable, and by
\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,\cdot)=D_{z}(\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,\cdot))\in\mathcal{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
u_{\lambda}
be a solution of the Hamiltonian boundary value problem

\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H({\lambda,t},u(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]\quad\hbox{and}\quad u(\tau)=Mu(0),

(1.3)

and
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto u_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is also continuous.

Under this assumption it follows from (
1.3
) that

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{u}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is also continuous.
For any given
\mu\in\Lambda
using nets we can prove
\|u_{\lambda}-u_{\mu}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
as
\lambda\to\mu
.
Note that Assumption
1.1
cannot guarantee that
each
u_{\lambda}:[0,\tau]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
is
C^{2}
since we have not assumed that
each
H(\lambda,\cdot,\cdot):[0,\tau]\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, is
C^{1}
,
and all possible partial derivatives of order one of
H
depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
.

Assumption 1.2
.

For a real
\tau>0
, a matrix
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
and
a topological space
\Lambda
, let

H:\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
each
H(\lambda,t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}
, is
C^{2}
,
and all possible partial derivatives of
H
depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, and that

\displaystyle H(\lambda,t+\tau,Mz)=H(\lambda,t,z)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}.

(1.4)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
v_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n}
satisfy the following generalized periodic Hamiltonian system

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},

(1.5)

and
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto v_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is also continuous.

(In some literatures solutions of (
1.5
) were called
(M,\tau)
-
periodic
or
affine
\tau
-periodic
.)
Clearly, a solution
v
of (
1.5
) gives one of (
1.3
),
v|_{[0,\tau]}
.
Conversely, since (
1.4
) implies

\displaystyle\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t+\tau,Mz)=(M^{-1})^{T}\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n},

(1.6)

every solution
u
of (
1.3
) gives rise to a solution
u^{M}
of (
1.5
)
defined by

u^{M}(t):=M^{k}u(t-k\tau)\;\;\hbox{for $k\tau<t\leq(k+1)\tau$},\;k\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

(1.7)

Moreover, this correspondence between solutions of (
1.3
) and (
1.5
) is one-to-one.

The linearized problems of (
1.3
) and (
1.5
) along
u_{\lambda}
and
v_{\lambda}
are, respectively,

\displaystyle\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,u_{\lambda}(t))u(t)\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]\quad\hbox{and}\quad u(\tau)=Mu(0),

(1.8)

\displaystyle\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))v(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(1.9)

Clearly, Assumption
1.1
and Assumption
1.2
imply that maps

\displaystyle\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,u_{\lambda}(t))\in\mathcal{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))\in\mathcal{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})

are continuous, respectively.

Under Assumption
1.2
let
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converge to
\mu\in\Lambda
, and let
\bar{v}_{k}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
be a solution of (
1.5
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}\in\Lambda
for each
k=1,2,\cdots
. In this paper we say that
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{\rm loc}
(resp.
C^{0}_{\rm loc}
)
if
\bar{v}_{k}|_{[a,b]}\to v_{\mu}|_{[a,b]}
in
C^{1}([a,b];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
(resp.
C^{0}([a,b];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
)
for any bounded interval
[a,b]\subset\mathbb{R}
.
Clearly,
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{\rm loc}
(resp.
C^{0}_{\rm loc}
) if and only if
\bar{v}_{k}|_{[0,\tau]}\to v_{\mu}|_{[0,\tau]}
in

C^{1}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
(resp.
C^{0}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
).
Because of these and the following Proposition
1.3
(i),
in the sense of Definition
C.2

(\mu,v_{\mu})
is said to be a
bifurcation point along sequences
of (
1.5
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu\in\Lambda

and solutions
\bar{v}_{k}\neq v_{\lambda_{k}}
of (
1.5
)
with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}\in\Lambda
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, such that

{\bar{v}_{k}}|_{[0,\tau]}\to{v_{\mu}}|_{[0,\tau]}
in
C^{0}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

or
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

Proposition 1.3
.

(i)

Under Assumption
1.1
,
(\mu,u_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of
the problem (
1.3
) in
\Lambda\times C^{0}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

with respect to
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
if and only if
it is that of the problem (
1.3
) in
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{E}:=E^{\frac{1}{2}}_{M}
be the Hilbert space as in Appendix
B
,
which is the fractional Sobolev space
H^{1/2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
if
M=I_{2n}
. Under Assumption
1.1
, if
H
also satisfies

|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)|\leq c_{1}+c_{2}|z|^{r}\quad\forall(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n},

(1.10)

where
c_{1},c_{2}\geq 0
and
r\in(1,2]
(resp.
r>1
) are constants if
M\neq I_{2n}
(resp.
M=I_{2n}
),
then
(\mu,u_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of the problem (
1.3
) in

\Lambda\times\mathbb{E}
with respect to
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
if and only if it
is that of the problem (
1.3
) in
\Lambda\times C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to

\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

This result will be proved in Appendix
B
.
It shows under Assumption
1.2
and the condition (
1.10
) that
statements of bifurcations along sequences of the problem (
1.5
) with
M=I_{2n}
are intrinsic,
i.e., are independent of choices of spaces

H^{1/2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
,
W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
and
C^{i}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
,
i=0,1
.

Let
\gamma_{\lambda}:[0,\tau]\to{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
be the fundamental matrix solution of

\dot{Z}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,u_{\lambda}(t))Z(t).

(1.11)

The Maslov type index of
\gamma_{\lambda}
relative to
M
is a pair of integers

(i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda}),\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda}))

(cf. Appendix
A
for details),
where
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})=\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)
is the dimension
of solution space of (
1.8
).
When
M=I_{2n}
,

(i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda}),\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda}))
is just the Maslov type index

(i_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda}),\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda}))

defined in
[
43
]
,
in particular,
i_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda})
is the Conley-Zehnder index
i_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{\lambda})
of
\gamma_{\lambda}

if
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda})=0
(
[
17
,
44
,
66
]
).
Consider the Banach subspaces of
C^{i}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
,

C^{i}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})=\{u\in C^{i}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\,|\,u(\tau)=Mu(0)\},\quad i\in{\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}.

For bifurcations of the boundary value problem (
1.3
) we have:

Theorem 1.4
.

Under Assumption
1.1
, let
\gamma_{\lambda}
be as above.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
if
(\mu,u_{\mu})
be a bifurcation point along sequences of
(
1.3
) in
\Lambda\times C^{0}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
, i.e.,
there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu
and
solutions
u^{k}\neq u_{\lambda_{k}}
of (
1.3
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}

such that
u^{k}\to u_{\mu}
in
C^{0}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Let
\Lambda
be first countable.
Suppose for some
\mu\in\Lambda
that
there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})=0
.
Let
\hat{\Lambda}:=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Then
(\mu,u_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of (
1.3
)
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}
.
In particular,
(\mu,u_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of (
1.3
)
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,u_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

(III)

(
Existence for bifurcations
):

Let
\Lambda
be path-connected. If
there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
,
then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
there exists a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}

and solutions
u^{k}\neq u_{\alpha(t_{k})}
of (
1.3
) with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
\|u^{k}-u_{\alpha(t_{k})}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
(and so
\|u^{k}-u_{\alpha(\bar{t})}\|_{C^{1}}\to 0
).
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
(resp.
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
).
In particular,
(\alpha(\bar{t}),0)
is a bifurcation point along sequences of (
1.3
)
in
\Lambda\times C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
.

From this theorem we immediately obtain:

Theorem 1.5
.

Under Assumption
1.2
, let
\gamma_{\lambda}
be
the fundamental matrix solution of

\dot{Z}(t)=J\nabla_{z}^{2}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))Z(t).

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
if
(\mu,v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of (
1.5
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Let
\Lambda
be first countable.
Suppose for some
\mu\in\Lambda
that
there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})]=\emptyset,

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})=0
.
Let
\hat{\Lambda}:=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Then
(\mu,v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of (
1.5
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(and so
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
).

(III)

(
Existence for bifurcations
):

Let
\Lambda
be path-connected. If there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
,
then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}

there exists a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}

and solutions
v^{k}\neq v_{\alpha(t_{k})}
of (
1.5
) with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
(v^{k})
converges to
v_{\alpha(\bar{t})}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
(resp.
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
).

Remark 1.6
.

(i)

Let us compare Theorem
1.5
(I) with previous related results.
If
M=I_{2n}
,
\tau=2\pi
and
\Lambda
is an open subset in a real finite dimensional Banach space,
the conclusion of Theorem
1.5
(I) can also be derived from
[
4
, Proposition 26.1]
provided that

\nabla_{z}H
and mappings
\Lambda\times[0,2\pi]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto v_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}

and
\Lambda\times[0,2\pi]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
are also
C^{1}
.
In fact, in the present conditions we can apply
[
4
, Proposition 26.1]
to
X=E=\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
k=1
and
f(\lambda,t,\xi)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,\xi+v_{\lambda}(t))-\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)

to conclude
\nu_{2\pi,M}(\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
because the assumption guarantees that

(\mu,0)
is also a bifurcation point of
\dot{x}=f(\lambda,t,x)
in
the sense of
[
4
, page 369]
.

Let
H\in C^{2}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})
be
2\pi
-periodic in the second variable
and satisfy
H(\lambda,t,0)\equiv 0
.
(Therefore it satisfies Assumption
1.2
with
\Lambda=[a,b]
and
M=I_{2n}
.) Suppose that

(H1)

There exist constants
r>1
and
c_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
, such that for all
(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,

|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)|\leq c_{1}+c_{2}|z|^{r}\quad\hbox{and}\quad|\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)|\leq c_{1}+c_{2}|z|^{r}.

(H2)

There is a path
\lambda\mapsto A_{\lambda}
of time-independent symmetric
2n\times 2n

real matrices such that

H(\lambda,t,z)=(A_{\lambda}z,z)_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}/2+R(\lambda,t,z)

where
\nabla_{z}R(\lambda,t,z)=o(|z|)
as
|z|\to 0
. (Without using
H(\lambda,t,0)\equiv 0
, this implies that

\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,0)=0
and
\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,0)=A_{\lambda}
for all
(\lambda,t)
. In particular,
\lambda\mapsto A_{\lambda}

is continuous because
H
is
C^{2}
.)

Let
\gamma_{\lambda}
be the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{Z}(t)=JA_{\lambda}Z(t)
.
It was claimed in
[
22
, page 22]
that
\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{\mu})=\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(2\pi)-I_{2n})\neq 0
is a necessary condition
for
(\mu,0)
to be a bifurcation point of (
1.5
) with respect to the trivial branch in

[a,b]\times H^{1/2}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
[or equivalently in
[a,b]\times C^{1}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

by Proposition
1.3
(ii)]. Clearly, this result is contained in Theorem
1.5
(I).

(ii)

Theorem
1.5
(III) with
M=I_{2n}
and
\tau=2\pi

generalizes
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
22
, Theorem 2.2]
.
Let
H\in C^{\infty}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})
be
2\pi
-periodic in the second variable
and satisfy the above growth condition (H1).
Assume that
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))
possesses a known family
\{v_{\lambda}\}
of
2\pi
-periodic solutions
smoothly parametrized by
\lambda
in
[a,b]
. Denote by
\gamma_{\lambda}
the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{Z}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))Z(t)
.
For each
\lambda\in[a,b]
, we define

L_{\lambda}\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H^{1/2}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}))
by

\displaystyle(L_{\lambda}v,w)_{H^{1/2}}=\int^{2\pi}_{0}(J\dot{v}(t)+\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))v(t),w(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}dt

as in the final line of
[
22
, page 24]
,
and
\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}:H^{1}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\to L^{2}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

by

(\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}v)(t)=J\dot{v}(t)+\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))v(t).

Let
{\rm sf}(\mathcal{A})
and
{\rm sf}(L)
be the spectral flows of the families
\{\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}\,|\,a\leq\lambda\leq b\}

and
\{L_{\lambda}\,|\,a\leq\lambda\leq b\}
, respectively.
Consider a symplectic path
\Gamma_{2\pi}:[a,b]\to{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
given by
\Gamma_{2\pi}(\lambda)=\gamma_{\lambda}(2\pi)
.
Its Maslov-type index
i(\Gamma_{2\pi},[a,b])
defined by (
A.1
) or (
A.4
)
is equal to
i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})-i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})
because using the continuous map

[a,b]\times[0,2\pi]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\gamma_{\lambda}(t)\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})

we can construct a homotopy from the product path
\Gamma_{2\pi}\ast\gamma_{a}
to
\gamma_{b}

with fixed end points in
{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
.
Let
\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})
be the Conley-Zehnder index of
\Gamma_{2\pi}
defined by
(
A.2
) (or
[
22
, (2.7)]
). Then (
A.8
) implies

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})=\mu_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{b})-\mu_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{a}).

(1.12)

By
[
66
]
(see also
[
11
, Theorem 11.1]
) and
[
22
, Proposition 2.1]
there holds

\displaystyle{\rm sf}(\mathcal{A})=\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})={\rm sf}(L)

(1.13)

and therefore
{\rm sf}(L)={\rm sf}(\mathcal{A})=\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})
(see also
[
69
, Theorem 4.1]
).
(
A.7
) may lead to

Claim
.
If
\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})=\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})=0
, then

\mu_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{\star})=i_{\rm CZ}(\gamma_{\star})=i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{\star})
,
\star=a,b
,
and so

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})=i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})-i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a}),

(1.14)

\displaystyle i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})\neq i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})\Longleftrightarrow\mu_{\rm CZ}(\Gamma_{2\pi})\neq 0\Longleftrightarrow{\rm sf}(L)\neq 0.

(1.15)

Having this
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
22
, Theorem 2.2]
may be restated as:

Theorem
(
[
22
, Theorem 1.1]
).
Let
H\in C^{2}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})
be
2\pi
-periodic in the second variable
and satisfy (H1)-(H2) in (i) above and

(H3)

The matrices
JA_{a}
and
JA_{b}
have no eigenvalues that are integral multiples of
\sqrt{-1}
, and therefore specially

\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})=\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})=0
.

If
i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})\neq i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})
, then there exists
\mu\in(a,b)
such that

(\mu,0)
is a bifurcation point of (
1.5
) with
(\Lambda,M,\tau)=([a,b],I_{2n},2\pi)

in
[a,b]\times H^{1/2}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to the trivial branch.

Theorem
(
[
22
, Theorem 2.2]
).
Let
H\in C^{\infty}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})

be
2\pi
-periodic in the second variable and satisfy (H1) in (i), and let
\{v_{\lambda}\,|\,\lambda\in[a,b]\}

be a family of
2\pi
-periodic solutions of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t))

smoothly parameterized by
\lambda
in
[a,b]
. If
\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})=\nu_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})=0
and

i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{b})\neq i_{2\pi}(\gamma_{a})
, then there exists
\mu\in(a,b)
such that

(\mu,v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of (
1.5
) with
(\Lambda,M,\tau)=([a,b],I_{2n},2\pi)

in
[a,b]\times H^{1/2}(S_{2\pi};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
.

When
v_{\lambda}\equiv 0
in this result, the condition “
H\in C^{\infty}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})
”
was weakened as
“
H\in C^{0}([a,b]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n})
, each
H(\lambda,\cdot,\cdot)
,
\lambda\in[a,b]
, is
C^{2}

and all possible partial derivatives of
H
depend continuously on the parameter
\lambda
”
in lines 1-4 of
[
69
, page 743]
.

Clearly, the assumptions on
H
in the above two theorems are stronger than those in Assumption
1.2

with
(\Lambda,M,\tau)=([a,b],I_{2n},2\pi)
.

To the best of the author’s knowledge, the following alternative bifurcation result
(and so its consequence, Theorem
1.8
) is completely new, and no similar results appear before.

Theorem 1.7

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type and of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let Assumption
1.1
with
\Lambda
being a real interval be satisfied,
and let
\gamma_{\lambda}
be as in Theorem
1.4
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Suppose for some interior point
\mu
of
\Lambda
that

\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-M)\neq 0
and
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)=0

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and that

i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})
takes, respectively, values
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})
and
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in
two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following assertions holds:

(i)

The problem (
1.3
) with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
u_{\mu}^{k}\neq u_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
u_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
\bar{u}_{\lambda}\neq u_{\lambda}
of
(
1.3
) with parameter value
\lambda
, such that

\bar{u}_{\lambda}-u_{\lambda}
converges to zero in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
u_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.3
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions
u_{\lambda}^{1}\neq u_{\lambda}
and
u_{\lambda}^{2}\neq u_{\lambda}
in
\mathcal{W}
,
which can also be required to satisfy

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{u}_{\lambda}^{1}(t),u^{1}_{\lambda}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,t,{u}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{u}^{2}_{\lambda}(t),u_{\lambda}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,t,{u}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))\right]dt

provided that
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})>1
and (
1.3
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions in
\mathcal{W}
.

Moreover, if
u_{\lambda}=0\;\forall\lambda
, and all
H(\lambda,t,\cdot)
are even, then either (i) holds or the following occurs:

(iv)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
),
(
1.3
) with parameter value
\lambda
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct pairs of nontrivial solutions,

\{u_{\lambda}^{i},-u_{\lambda}^{i}\}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
which converge to zero in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

As above this directly leads to the following bifurcation result of the system (
1.5
).

Theorem 1.8

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type and of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let Assumption
1.2
with
\Lambda
being a real interval be satisfied,
and let
\gamma_{\lambda}
be as in Theorem
1.5
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Suppose for some interior point
\mu
of
\Lambda
that

\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-M)\neq 0
and
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)=0

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and that

i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})
takes, respectively, values
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})
and
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in
two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then one of the following assertions holds:

(i)

The problem (
1.5
) with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of solutions,
v_{\mu}^{k}\neq v_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
v_{\mu}^{k}|_{[0,\tau]}\to v_{\mu}|_{[0,\tau]}
in
C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
\bar{v}_{\lambda}\neq v_{\lambda}
of
(
1.5
) with parameter value
\lambda

such that
\bar{v}_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}-v_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}\to 0
in
C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iii)

For a given neighborhood
\mathcal{W}
of
v_{\mu}|_{[0,\tau]}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.5
) with parameter value
\lambda

has at least two distinct solutions
v_{\lambda}^{1}\neq v_{\lambda}
and
v_{\lambda}^{2}\neq v_{\lambda}
such that

v_{\lambda}^{1}|_{[0,\tau]}
and
v_{\lambda}^{2}|_{[0,\tau]}
are in
\mathcal{W}
,
which can also be required to satisfy

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t),v^{1}_{\lambda}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,t,{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{v}^{2}_{\lambda}(t),v_{\lambda}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,t,{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))\right]dt

provided that
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})>1
and (
1.5
) with parameter value
\lambda

has only finitely many solutions whose restrictions to
[0,\tau]
belong to
\mathcal{W}
.

Moreover, if
v_{\lambda}=0\;\forall\lambda
, and all
H(\lambda,t,\cdot)
are even, then either (i) holds or the following occurs:

(iv)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
),
(
1.5
) with parameter value
\lambda
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct pairs of nontrivial solutions,

\{v_{\lambda}^{i},-v_{\lambda}^{i}\}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
such that their restrictions to
[0,\tau]
converge to zero in
C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\lambda\to\mu
.

The first part is an alternative bifurcation result of Rabinowitz’s type,
and the second part is of alternative bifurcations of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type.

Theorem
1.7
has the following:

Corollary 1.9
.

Let
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
, and let
H_{0},\hat{H}:[0,\tau]\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}

be
C^{1}
functions such that all
H_{0}(t,\cdot),\hat{H}(t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
are
C^{2}
and all their
partial derivatives depend continuously on
(t,z)\in[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
.
Let
\bar{u}:[0,\tau]\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
satisfy

\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H_{0}(t,u(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]
and
u(\tau)=Mu(0)
.

Suppose that
\nabla_{z}\hat{H}(t,\bar{u}(t))=0
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
and that

either
\nabla^{2}_{z}\hat{H}(t,\bar{u}(t))>0\;\forall t
or
\nabla_{z}^{2}\hat{H}(t,\bar{u}(t))<0\;\forall t
.

Take
H(\lambda,t,z)=H_{0}(t,z)+\lambda\hat{H}(t,z)
and
\Lambda={\mathbb{R}}
in (
1.3
).
Then for the fundamental matrix solution
\gamma_{\lambda}
of

\dot{Z}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,\bar{u}(t))Z(t)
,

\Sigma:=\{\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\,|\,\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})>0\}

is a discrete set in
{\mathbb{R}}
,
and
(\mu,\bar{u})\in{\mathbb{R}}\times W^{1,2}_{M}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})

is a bifurcation point for (
1.3
) with this
H

if and only if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})>0
.
Moreover, for each
\mu\in\Sigma

and a small enough
\rho>0
it holds that

\displaystyle i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})&\forall\lambda\in[\mu-\rho,\mu),\\
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})&\forall\lambda\in(\mu,\mu+\rho]\end{array}\right.

if
\nabla_{z}^{2}\hat{H}(t,\bar{u}(t))>0\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]
, and

\displaystyle i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})&\forall\lambda\in[\mu-\rho,\mu),\\
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})&\forall\lambda\in(\mu,\mu+\rho]\end{array}\right.

if
\nabla_{z}^{2}\hat{H}(t,\bar{u}(t))<0\;\forall t\in[0,\tau]
.
Consequently, for each
\mu\in\Sigma
,
the conclusions in the first part of Theorem
1.7

holds, and the second one of Theorem
1.7
is also true provided that

\bar{u}=0
and all
H_{0}(t,\cdot),\hat{H}(t,\cdot)
are even.

If
\bar{u}
is constant and
H_{0}=0
, Proposition
A.5
gives stronger results than (
1.9
) and (
1.9
).

As a consequence of Theorem
1.7

we can also obtain a corresponding result to
[
51
, Theorem 5.6]
.

Corollary 1.10
.

For a real
\tau>0
let
H:[0,\tau]\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
C^{1}
function such that each

H(t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
t\in[0,\tau]
, is
C^{2}
and all partial derivatives of
H(t,\cdot)
depend continuously on

(t,z)\in[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
. Let
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
,

\bar{u}\in{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})
satisfy
\nabla_{z}H(t,\bar{u})=0
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
, and let

B(t)=\nabla^{2}_{z}{H}(t,\bar{u})
,
\xi_{2n}(t)=I_{2n}
for
t\in[0,\tau]
, and
\Upsilon_{B}
be
the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{Z}(t)=JB(t)Z(t)
on
[0,\tau]
.

(I)

Suppose that
B(t)>0
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
and
i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{B})\neq i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})+\dim{\rm Ker}(I_{2n}-M)
.
Then there exists at least one and at most finitely many numbers
in
(0,\tau)
,
\tau_{1}<\cdots<\tau_{l}
, such that

\nu_{\tau_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{B}):=\dim{\rm Ker}(\Upsilon_{B}(\tau_{k})-M)\neq 0,\quad k=1,\cdots,l,

and that for each
\tau_{k}
one of the following assertions holds:

(I-1)

The boundary value problem

\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\frac{\tau_{k}}{\tau}t,u(t))\;\forall t\in[0,\tau_{k}]
and
u(\tau_{k})=Mu(0)

has a sequence of solutions converging to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,\tau_{k}];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
,
u_{m}\neq\bar{u}
,
m=1,2,\cdots
.

(I-2)

For every
s\neq\tau_{k}
near
\tau_{k}
,
the boundary value problem

\dot{u}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\frac{s}{\tau}t,u(t))\;\forall t\in[0,s]
and
u(s)=Mu(0)

(1.22)

has a solution
u_{s}\neq\bar{u}
, which
converges to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,s];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
s\to\tau_{k}
.

(I-3)

There is an one-sided neighborhood
\Delta^{0}
of
\tau_{k}
in
[0,\tau]
such that
for any
s\in\Delta^{0}\setminus\{\tau_{k}\}
, (
1.22
)
has at least two distinct solutions
u_{s}^{1}\neq\bar{u}
and
u_{s}^{2}\neq\bar{u}
near
\bar{u}
,
which converges to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([0,s];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
s\to\tau_{k}
; besides,

u_{s}^{1}
and
u_{s}^{2}
can also be required to satisfy

\int^{s}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{u}_{s}^{1}(t),u^{1}_{s}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\frac{s}{\tau}t,{u}_{s}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{s}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{u}^{2}_{s}(t),u_{s}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\frac{s}{\tau}t,{u}_{s}^{2}(t))\right]dt

provided that
\nu_{\tau_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{B})>1
and (
1.22
)
has only finitely many solutions near
\bar{u}
.

Furthermore, if
\bar{u}=0
and all
H(t,\cdot)
are even, then either (I-1) holds or the following occurs:

(I-4)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Delta^{-}
and
\Delta^{+}
of
\tau_{k}
in
[0,\tau]

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\tau_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{B})
,
and for
s\in\Delta^{-}\setminus\{\tau_{k}\}
(resp.
s\in\Delta^{+}\setminus\{\tau_{k}\}
),
(
1.22
) has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct pairs of nontrivial solutions,

\{u_{s}^{i},-u_{s}^{i}\}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
which converge to zero in
C^{1}_{M}([0,s];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
s\to\tau_{k}
.

(II)

Suppose that

B(t)<0
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
and that

i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})\neq i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})+\dim{\rm Ker}(I_{2n}-M)
, where

\Upsilon_{\hat{B}}(t)=\Upsilon_{B}(\tau-t)\Upsilon_{B}(\tau)^{-1}
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
.
Then there exists at least one and at most finitely many numbers
in
(0,\tau)
,
\delta_{1}<\cdots<\delta_{l}
, such that

\nu_{\delta_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}}):=\dim{\rm Ker}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}}(\delta_{k})-M)\neq 0,\quad k=1,\cdots,l,

and that for each
\delta_{k}
one of the following assertions holds:

(II-1)

The boundary value problem

\dot{w}(t)=\frac{\tau}{\delta_{k}}J\nabla_{z}{H}(t,w(t))\;\forall t\in[\tau-\delta_{k}^{2}/\tau,\tau]
and
Mw(\tau)=w(\tau-\delta_{k}^{2}/\tau)

has a sequence of solutions,
w_{m}\neq\bar{u}
,
m=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([\tau-\delta_{k}^{2}/\tau,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

(II-2)

For every
\rho\neq\delta_{k}
near
\delta_{k}
,
the boundary value problem

\dot{w}(t)=\frac{\tau}{\rho}J\nabla_{z}{H}(t,w(t))\;\forall t\in[\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau,\tau]
and
Mw(\tau)=w(\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau)

(1.23)

has a solution
w_{\rho}\neq\bar{u}
, which
converges to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\rho\to\delta_{k}
.

(II-3)

There is an one-sided neighborhood
\Delta^{0}
of
\delta_{k}
in
[0,\tau]
such that
for any
\rho\in\Delta^{0}\setminus\{\tau_{k}\}
, (
1.23
)
has at least two distinct solutions
w_{\rho}^{1}\neq\bar{u}
and
w_{\rho}^{2}\neq\bar{u}
near
\bar{u}
,
which converges to
\bar{u}
in
C^{1}_{M}([\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\rho\to\tau_{k}
; besides,

w_{\rho}^{1}
and
w_{\rho}^{2}
can also be required to satisfy

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{w}_{\rho}^{1}(t),w^{1}_{\rho}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+\frac{\tau}{\rho}H(t,{w}_{\rho}^{1}(t))\right]dt

\displaystyle\neq\int^{\tau}_{\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{w}^{2}_{\rho}(t),w_{\rho}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+\frac{\tau}{\rho}H(t,{u}_{s}^{2}(t))\right]dt

provided that
\nu_{\delta_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})>1
and (
1.23
)
has only finitely many solutions near
\bar{u}
.

Furthermore, if
\bar{u}=0
and all
H(t,\cdot)
are even, then either (II-1) holds or the following occurs:

(II-4)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Delta^{-}
and
\Delta^{+}
of
\delta_{k}
in
[0,\tau]

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\delta_{k},M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})
,
and for
\rho\in\Delta^{-}\setminus\{\delta_{k}\}
(resp.
\rho\in\Delta^{+}\setminus\{\delta_{k}\}
),
(
1.23
) has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
) distinct pairs of nontrivial solutions,

\{w_{\rho}^{i},-u_{\rho}^{i}\}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
which converge to zero in
C^{1}_{M}([\tau-\rho^{2}/\tau,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
as
\rho\to\delta_{k}
.

Clearly, (I) of Corollary
1.10
always implies that there exists a sequence
(s_{m})\subset(0,\tau)
such that
(
1.22
) with
s=s_{m}
has a solution
u_{m}\neq\bar{u}
for each
m
, and that

\|u_{m}-\bar{u}\|_{C^{1}([0,s_{m}];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})}\to 0
as
m\to\infty
. By (II) of Corollary
1.10

we have also a similar conclusion.

Remark 1.11
.

(1)

By
[
19
, Remark 4.3]
, the conditions
in Corollary
1.10

“
i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{B})\neq i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})+\dim{\rm Ker}(I_{2n}-M)
”
and

“
i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})\neq i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})+\dim{\rm Ker}(I_{2n}-M)
”

respectively become

“
i_{\tau}(\Upsilon_{B})\neq n
” and “
i_{\tau}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})\neq n
”

if
M=I_{2n}
, and

“
i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{B})\neq\kappa
” and “
i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{\hat{B}})\neq\kappa
”

if
M={\rm diag}\{-I_{n-\kappa},I_{\kappa},-I_{n-\kappa},I_{\kappa}\}
.

(2)

If
H
in Corollary
1.10
is independent of time, then

B(t)\equiv\nabla^{2}_{z}{H}(\bar{u})
,
\Upsilon_{B}(t)=\exp(tJ\nabla^{2}_{z}{H}(\bar{u}))
and

\Upsilon_{\hat{B}}(t)=\Upsilon_{B}(\tau-t)\Upsilon_{B}(\tau)^{-1}=\exp(-tJ\nabla^{2}_{z}{H}(\bar{u}))
for all
t\in[0,\tau]
.

Corollary
1.9
implies

Corollary 1.12
.

For
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
, let
C^{1}
functions
H_{0},\hat{H}:{\mathbb{R}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
satisfy:

(a)

H_{0}(t+\tau,Mx)=H_{0}(t,x)
and
\hat{H}(t+\tau,Mx)=\hat{H}(t,x)
for all
(t,x)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
.

(b)

all
H_{0}(t,\cdot),\hat{H}(t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
are
C^{2}
and all their
partial derivatives depend continuously on
(t,z)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
.

Let
\bar{v}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
satisfy

(c)

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H_{0}(t,v(t))
and
v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\forall t
.

(d)

\nabla_{z}\hat{H}(t,\bar{v}(t))=0
for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
, and either
\nabla^{2}_{z}\hat{H}(t,\bar{v}(t))>0\;\forall t
or
\nabla^{2}_{z}\hat{H}(t,\bar{v}(t))<0\;\forall t
.

Take
H(\lambda,t,z)=H_{0}(t,z)+\lambda\hat{H}(t,z)
in (
1.4
).
Then

(A)

\Sigma:=\{\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\,|\,\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})>0\}

is a discrete set in
{\mathbb{R}}
.

(B)

(\mu,\bar{v})
with
\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}
is a bifurcation point for (
1.5
)
if and only if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})>0
.

(C)

For each
\mu\in\Sigma
and a small enough
\rho>0
, (
1.9
) and (
1.9
)
hold, and therefore the conclusions in the first part of Theorem
1.8

holds, and the second one of Theorem
1.8
is also true provided that

\bar{v}=0
and all
H_{0}(t,\cdot),\hat{H}(t,\cdot)
are even.

1.2.
Bifurcations for generalized periodic solutions of autonomous Hamiltonian systems

Assumption 1.13
.

Let
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
be an orthogonal symplectic matrix and
\Lambda
be
a topological space. Moreover, let

H:\Lambda\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that each

H_{\lambda}:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, is
M
-invariant and
C^{2}
, and all its partial derivatives depend continuously on
the parameter
\lambda\in\Lambda
. For some fixed real
\tau>0

and each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
v_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n}
satisfy the following generalized periodic Hamiltonian system

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,v(t))\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},

(1.24)

and
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto v_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is also continuous.

Under this assumption, each
v_{\lambda}
is
C^{2}
, and

\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
and
\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\ddot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
are also continuous.
Moreover, each element in
{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\lambda}:=\{v_{\lambda}(\theta+\cdot)\,|\,\theta\in{\mathbb{R}}\}
(
{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit
)
also satisfies (
1.24
). It follows that

\hbox{$\ddot{v}_{\lambda}(t)=J\nabla_{z}^{2}H({\lambda},v_{\lambda})\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)$\quad and\quad$\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t+\tau)=M\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\;\forall t$}.

(1.25)

Thus if
v_{\mu}
is nonconstant (i.e.,
\dot{v}_{\mu}\neq 0
) for some
\mu\in\Lambda
,
then
(\mu,v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of (
1.24
)
in the sense above (
1.10
).
In order to give an exact description for bifurcation pictures of solutions of (
1.24
) near
{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\lambda}

some concepts are needed. Two solutions
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
of (
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda

is said to be
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct
if they belong to different
{\mathbb{R}}
-orbits.
We call
(\mu,{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\mu})
a
bifurcation
{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit along sequences
of (
1.24
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu
, and a solution
\bar{v}_{k}
of (
1.24
)
with parameter value
\lambda_{k}
for each
k
,
such that: (i)
\bar{v}_{k}\notin{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\lambda_{k}}\;\forall k
, (ii) all
\bar{v}_{k}
are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct,
(iii)
\bar{v}_{k}\to v_{\mu}
in
C^{1}_{\rm loc}
.

When each
v_{\lambda}
is constant, we have the following
Theorem
1.14
and Corollaries
1.15
,
1.16
.

Theorem 1.14

(
Alternative bifurcations of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type and of Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let Assumption
1.13
with
\Lambda
being a real interval be satisfied.
Suppose that the orthogonal symplectic matrix
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})

satisfies
M^{l}=I_{2n}
for some integer
l\geq 1
, and that
each
v_{\lambda}
is constant, (hence viewed as a constant vector in

{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})\cap\{dH_{\lambda}=0\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
).
Let
\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\exp(tJ\nabla_{z}^{2}H({\lambda},v_{\lambda}))
, i.e.,
the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H({\lambda},v_{\lambda})v(t)
.
For some interior point
\mu
of
\Lambda
, assume that the following two conditions are satisfied:

(a)

The following problem

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}^{2}H({\mu},v_{\mu})v(t)
and
v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\forall t

(1.26)

has no nonzero constant solutions, i.e.,
{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})\cap{\rm Ker}(\nabla_{z}^{2}H({\mu},v_{\mu}))=\{0\}
.

(b)

\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-M)\neq 0
,
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)=0

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and

i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})
takes, respectively, values
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})
and
i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.

Then one of the following alternatives occurs for (
1.24
):

(i)

The problem (
1.24
) with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions,

\bar{v}_{k}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\mu}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which converges to
v_{\mu}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology.

(ii)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})/2
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
),
(
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct
solutions,
v_{\lambda}^{i}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\lambda}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
such that all
v_{\lambda}^{i}-v_{\lambda}
converge to zero on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

In particular,
(\mu,v_{\mu})=(\mu,{\mathbb{R}}\cdot v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation
{\mathbb{R}}
-orbit of (
1.24
).
Moreover, if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\mu})\geq 3
, then (ii) may be replaced by the following alternatives:

(iii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a
solution
\bar{v}_{\lambda}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\lambda}
of
(
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda
, such that
\bar{v}_{\lambda}-v_{\lambda}
converges to zero on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(iv)

For a given
\varepsilon>0
there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda

has either infinitely many
\mathbb{R}
-distinct solutions
\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\lambda}

such that
\|\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}|_{[0,\tau]}-v_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}\|_{C^{1}}<\varepsilon
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, or
at least two
\mathbb{R}
-distinct solutions
\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{1}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\lambda}
and
\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot v_{\lambda}
such that
\|\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{i}|_{[0,\tau]}-v_{\lambda}|_{[0,\tau]}\|_{C^{1}}<\varepsilon
,
i=1,2
,
and that

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{\hat{v}}_{\lambda}^{1}(t),\hat{v}^{1}_{\lambda}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{\hat{v}}^{2}_{\lambda}(t),\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))\right]dt.

The first part is an alternative bifurcation result of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type,
and the second part is that of Rabinowitz’s type.

By the proof of Corollary
1.9
we may derive from Theorem
1.14
:

Corollary 1.15
.

Let
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
be as in Theorem
1.14
,

\bar{v}\in{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})
, and
H_{0},\hat{H}:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be
M
-invariant
C^{2}
-functions.
Suppose that
dH_{0}(\bar{v})=d\hat{H}(\bar{v})=0
and
that either
\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})>0
or
\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})<0
.
Then for
\gamma_{\lambda}(t)=\exp(tJH_{0}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})+\lambda tJ\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))
and any
\tau>0
,

\Sigma_{\tau}:=\{\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\,|\,\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})>0\}
is a discrete set in
{\mathbb{R}}
;
and for each
\mu\in\Sigma_{\tau}
such that
(
1.26
) with
H(\mu,x)=H_{0}(x)+\mu\hat{H}(x)
and
v_{\mu}=\bar{v}
has no nonzero constant solutions,
the conclusions of Theorem
1.14

holds for (
1.24
) with
H(\lambda,x)=H_{0}(x)+\lambda\hat{H}(x)
and
v_{\lambda}\equiv\bar{v}\;\forall\lambda
.

In particular, taking
\tau=1
,
H_{0}=0
and
H=\hat{H}
we obtain
the following existence result of
M
-rotating periodic orbits of
\dot{v}=J\nabla H(v)
near a
M
-equilibruim
\bar{v}
.

Corollary 1.16
.

Let
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
and
\bar{v}\in{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})
be as in Corollary
1.15
,
and let
H:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
M
-invariant
C^{2}
-function satisfying

dH(\bar{v})=0
. Suppose that either
{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})>0
or
{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})<0
.
Then

(A)

\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M):=\{\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\,|\,\nu_{1,M}(\gamma_{\lambda})=\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\lambda J{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))-M)>0\}

is a discrete set.

(B)

If
{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})>0
and
\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)\cap(0,\infty)\neq\emptyset

(resp.
{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})<0
and
\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)\cap(-\infty,0)\neq\emptyset
),
then for
\mu\in\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)\cap(0,\infty)
(resp.
\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)\cap(-\infty,0)
),
one of the following alternatives occurs:

(B.i)

The problem

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla H(v(t))\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(t+\mu)=Mv(t),\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}

(1.27)

has a sequence of
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions,
v_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that each
v_{k}
is
\mathbb{R}
-distinct from
\bar{v}
, and that
(v_{k})
converges to

\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology.

(B.ii)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\frac{1}{2}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\mu{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))-M)
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
)
the problem

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla H(v(t))\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(t+\lambda)=Mv(t),\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}}

(1.28)

has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions
solutions,
v_{\lambda}^{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
such that each of them is
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct from
\bar{v}
and converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval

I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

Moreover, if
\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\mu{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))-M)\geq 3
,
then either (B.i) holds or one of the following alternatives occurs:

(B.iii)

For every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
,
the problem (
1.28
) has a solution
v_{\lambda}
, which is
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct from
\bar{v}
and converges to
\bar{v}

on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(B.iv)

For a given
\varepsilon>0
there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
in
{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, the problem (
1.28
) with parameter value
\lambda

has either infinitely many
\mathbb{R}
-distinct solutions
\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}

such that each of them is
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct from
\bar{v}
and
\|\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}|_{[0,|\lambda|]}-\bar{v}\|_{C^{1}}<\varepsilon
,
k=1,2,\cdots
, or
at least two
\mathbb{R}
-distinct solutions
\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{1}
and
\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}

such that:
a)
each of both is
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct from
\bar{v}
,
b)

\|\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{i}|_{[0,|\lambda|]}-\bar{v}\|_{C^{1}}<\varepsilon
,
i=1,2
,

c)

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{\hat{v}}_{\lambda}^{1}(t),\hat{v}^{1}_{\lambda}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{\hat{v}}^{2}_{\lambda}(t),\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,\hat{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))\right]dt.

Remark 1.17
.

Suppose
H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})>0
. By Williamson theorem (cf.
[
29
, page 41]
) there exists a symplectic matrix
S\in{\rm Sp}(2n,{\mathbb{R}})
such that

S^{T}H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})S=D_{H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})}={\rm diag}(\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n},\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n})
,
where
0<\varrho_{j}\leq\varrho_{k}
for
j\leq k
, are the symplectic eigenvalues of
H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})
, namely
(\pm\sqrt{-1}\varrho_{j})
for
1\leq j\leq n

are all eigenvalues of
JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})
. Therefore we can reduce the study of the Hamiltonian system
\dot{v}=J\nabla H(v)

with
M
-rotating periods near the
M
-equilibruim
\bar{v}

to that of the Hamiltonian system
\dot{v}=J\nabla G(v)
with
S^{-1}MS
-rotating periods near
\bar{w}=S^{-1}\bar{v}
, where
G(z)=H(Sz)

and so
G^{\prime}(\bar{w})=S^{T}H^{\prime}(S\bar{w})=0
and

G^{\prime\prime}(\bar{w})=S^{T}H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})S=D_{H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})}
. Since
S
is symplectic,
JG^{\prime\prime}(\bar{w})=S^{-1}JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})S
and

\exp(\lambda JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))=S\exp(\lambda JD_{H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})})S^{-1}
for any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.
The former implies that both
G^{\prime\prime}(\bar{w})
and
H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})
have the same symplectic eigenvalues.
The latter leads to

\displaystyle\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)\cap(0,\infty)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\lambda>0\,|\,\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\lambda JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))-M)>0\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(G,\bar{w},S^{-1}MS)\cap(0,\infty).

In particular, from this and (
1.52
) we derive

\displaystyle\Gamma(H,\bar{v},I_{2n})\cap(0,\infty)=\cup^{n}_{j=1}\left(\frac{2\pi}{\varrho_{j}}\mathbb{N}\right).

because
\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\lambda JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v}))-I_{2n})
is equal to

\displaystyle\dim{\rm Ker}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\rm diag}(\cos(\varrho_{1}\lambda),...,\cos(\varrho_{n}\lambda))&-{\rm diag}(\sin(\varrho_{1}\lambda),...,\sin(\varrho_{n}\lambda))\\
{\rm diag}(\sin(\varrho_{1}\lambda),...,\sin(\varrho_{n}\lambda))&{\rm diag}(\cos(\varrho_{1}\lambda),...,\cos(\varrho_{n}\lambda))\\
\end{array}\right)-I_{2n}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{n}_{j=1}\dim{\rm Ker}\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\cos(\varrho_{j}\lambda)&-\sin(\varrho_{j}\lambda)\\
\sin(\varrho_{j}\lambda)&\cos(\varrho_{j}\lambda)\end{array}\right)-I_{2}\right).

This is exactly the set of periods of all period solutions of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})v
.

Let us compare previous results with Corollary
1.16
.
Very recently, J. Xing, X. Yang and Y. Li
[
70
, Corollary 1.2]

gave a Lyapunov type theorem on
M
-rotating periodic orbits for an orthogonal
M\in{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
,
which can be stated as follows in our notations:

Let
\bar{v}\in{\rm Ker}(M-I_{2n})
,
and let
H:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a
M
-invariant
C^{2}
-function satisfying

H^{\prime}(\bar{v})=0
and
{H}^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})>0
. Suppose that the symplectic eigenvalues of
H^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})
as above,

\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n}
, are nonresonant, i.e.,
\varrho_{i}/\varrho_{j}\notin\mathbb{Q}
for
1\leq i,j\leq n
,
i\neq j
. Then
for each sufficiently small
\varepsilon>0
, the energy surface

H(v)=H(\bar{v})+\varepsilon^{2}
contains at least
n

M
-rotating periodic orbits of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime}(v)

with periods close to ones of the linear system
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})v
.

Note that the set of
M
-rotating periods of the linear system
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(\bar{v})v
is exactly
the discrete set
\Gamma(H,\bar{v},M)
in Corollary
1.16
(A).
This theorem and Corollary
1.16
belong to
two categories of fixed energy and fixed period problems about
M
-rotating periodic orbits, respectively.

Previous other related results require
M=I_{2n}
.
To compare with them we may assume
\bar{v}=0
and

H^{\prime\prime}(0)={\rm diag}(\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n},\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n})
by the above arguments.
Then for
T>0
,
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
has a nontrivial
T
-periodic solution if and only if

T
belongs to
\cup^{n}_{j=1}\left(\frac{2\pi}{\varrho_{j}}\mathbb{N}\right)
. For
1\leq i\leq n
let

\mathbb{R}^{2}_{i}=\{(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n},p_{1},\cdots,p_{n})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}\,|\,q_{j}=p_{j}=0\;\forall j\neq i\}.

It is an invariant subspace of
JH^{\prime\prime}(0)
. For a given
T\in\cup^{n}_{j=1}\left(\frac{2\pi}{\varrho_{j}}\mathbb{N}\right)
,

E=\oplus_{T\varrho_{i}\in 2\pi\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{R}^{2}_{i}
and
F=\oplus_{T\varrho_{i}\notin 2\pi\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{R}^{2}_{i}

are invariant subspaces of
JH^{\prime\prime}(0)
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2n}=E\oplus F
. It is easy to see:

\bullet

\dim E=2\sharp\{j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\,|\,T\varrho_{j}\in 2\pi\mathbb{N}\}=\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(TJH^{\prime\prime}(0))-I_{2n})
[by (
1.17
)].

\bullet

Each solution of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
in
E
is
T
-periodic, and
no solutions of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
with initial data in
F\setminus\{0\}
is
T
-periodic.

Since
H^{\prime\prime}(0)>0
,
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
has no constant solutions in
E\setminus\{0\}
and
the quadratic form
E\ni z\mapsto(H^{\prime\prime}(0)z,z)
has the signature
\dim E
. From

[
21
, Theorem 8.4]
(or
[
23
, Theorem]
) we can only derive:

either (B.i) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
holds, or
(B.ii) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
is true.

But when
2\sharp\{j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\,|\,T\varrho_{j}\in 2\pi\mathbb{N}\}\geq 3
,
Corollary
1.16
can also give:

either (B.i) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
holds, or
(B.iii) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
or (B.iv) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
is true.

Moreover,
[
68
, Corollaries 4.2,4.3]
can only lead to:
for each
1\leq j\leq n
there exists a sequence
(v_{k})
of nonconstant
periodic solutions of
\dot{v}=J\nabla H(v)
with (not necessarily minimal) period
T_{k}
such that

\|z_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\to 0
and
T_{k}\to 2\pi/\varrho_{j}
.

Assume
\{\varrho_{1},\cdots,\varrho_{n}\}=\{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{p}\}
with
\beta_{1}<\cdots<\beta_{p}
.
For
\beta\in\{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{p}\}
let

H_{\beta}
be the sum of the generalized eigenspaces of
JH^{\prime\prime}(0)
in
\mathbb{C}^{2n}

associated to the eigenvalues of the form
\pm ik\beta
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.,

H_{\beta}=\sum_{\lambda\in\Gamma_{\beta}}\cup^{\infty}_{l=1}{\rm Ker}(\lambda I_{2n}-JH^{\prime\prime}(0))
,
where
\Gamma_{\beta}=\{ik\beta\,|\,k\in\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}\}\cap\{-i\beta_{1},\cdots,-i\beta_{p},i\beta_{1},\cdots,i\beta_{p}\}
.
Put

\Xi_{\beta}=\{\xi,\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}\,|\,\xi+i\eta\in H_{\beta}\}.

They are invariant subspaces of
JH^{\prime\prime}(0)
in
\mathbb{C}^{2n}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2n}
, respectively.

\dim\Xi_{\beta}
is even and
H^{\prime\prime}(0)|_{\Xi_{\beta}}>0
implies: a)
each solution of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
with initial value in
\Xi_{\beta}
has (not necessary minimal) period
2\pi/\beta
and
no solutions of
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
with initial data in
\mathbb{R}^{2n}\setminus\Xi_{\beta}
has the period
2\pi/\beta
;
b)
\dot{v}=JH^{\prime\prime}(0)v
has
\dim\Xi_{\beta}/2
linearly independent periodic solutions with period
2\pi/\beta

as claimed below (H2) on the page 128 of
[
5
]
. The latter sentence shows
\dim\Xi_{\beta}=\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(TJH^{\prime\prime}(0))-I_{2n})

with period
T=2\pi/\beta
. Therefore
using
[
5
, Theorem 9.5]
we can only obtain:

For each
\beta\in\{\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{p}\}
and
T=2\pi/\beta
,
either (B.i) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
holds, or
(B.ii) with
(\bar{v},\mu,M)=(0,T,I_{2n})
is true.

[
5
, Theorem 9.5]
gave no conclusions for
T\in\cup^{n}_{j=1}\left(\frac{2\pi}{\varrho_{j}}\mathbb{N}\right)\setminus\{2\pi/\beta_{k}\,|\,k=1,\cdots,p\}
.

In summary, Corollary
1.16
with
M=I_{2n}
cannot be included by the previous results.

Most of the general works on bifurcations of Hamiltonian systems have very little on the case where all
v_{\lambda}

in Assumption
1.13
are equal to a nonequilibrium (i.e., nonconstant) solution
\bar{v}
.
Using the iso-energetic Poincaré mapping some results were obtained, see
[
59
,
26
]
.
Here are our three bifurcation results of
M
-rotating periodic solutions from
\bar{v}
.

Theorem 1.18

(
Necessary condition
)
.

Let
\Lambda,M
and
H
be as in Assumption
1.13
, and let

\bar{v}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
be a nonconstant solution of (
1.24
) for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Suppose that for some
\mu\in\Lambda

there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu
such that
(
1.24
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
has solutions
v_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
which are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct each other and satisfies

v_{k}|_{[0,\tau]}\to\bar{v}|_{[0,\tau]}
in
C^{0}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.
Then the following problem

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H({\mu},\bar{v}(t))v(t)
and
v(t+\tau)=Mv(t)\;\forall t

(1.34)

has at least two linearly independent nontrivial solutions, i.e.,

\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-M)\geq 2
, where
\gamma_{\lambda}(t)
is the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,\bar{v}(t))v(t)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
1.18
, since
\dot{\bar{v}}(0)\neq 0
and

\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)\dot{\bar{v}}(0)=\dot{\bar{v}}(\tau)=M\dot{\bar{v}}(0)
we get
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)\geq 1
.
(Though
1
and
-1
as eigenvalues of a symplectic matrix
S\in{\rm Sp}(2n;\mathbb{R})
always have even algebraic multiplicity it is possible
that the geometric multiplicity of
1
(or
-1
) is equal to one.)
When
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-M)=1
we call
\bar{v}
a
transversally nondegenerate

M
-rotating
\tau
-periodic solution of (
1.24
). Therefore the conclusion of Theorem
1.18

says
\bar{v}
to be a transversally degenerate
M
-rotating
\tau
-periodic solution of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H({\mu},v(t))
.

Clearly, Theorem
1.18
is a strengthen version of Theorem
1.5
(a).
Corresponding with (b) and (c) of Theorem
1.5
and Corollary
1.12
respectively, we have also:

Theorem 1.19

(
Sufficient condition
)
.

Let
\Lambda,M
,
H
,
\bar{v}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}
be as in Theorem
1.18
,
and let
\Lambda
be first countable.
Suppose:

(a)

The orbit
{\cal O}:={\mathbb{R}}\cdot\bar{v}
is an embedded circle
(i.e.,
\mathbb{R}_{\bar{v}}
is an infinite cyclic subgroup of
{\mathbb{R}}
with generator
p>0
).

(b)

For some
\mu\in\Lambda
,
\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-M)\geq 2
and
there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})-1]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})-1]=\emptyset,

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})=1
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})=1
.

(c)

For any solution
v
of (
1.24
) with
\lambda=\mu
,
if there exists a sequence
(s_{k})
of reals such that
s_{k}\cdot v

converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{0}
-topology (and so
C^{1}
-topology by Proposition
1.3
), then
v
is periodic.
(Clearly, this holds if
M^{l}=I_{2n}
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}
.)

Then there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\hat{\Lambda}:=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
converging to
\mu
and
solutions
v_{k}
of (
1.24
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that any two of these
v_{k}
are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct and that

(v_{k})
converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.

Theorem 1.20

(
Existence for bifurcations
)
.

Let
\Lambda,M
,
H
,
\bar{v}
and
\gamma_{\lambda}
be as in Theorem
1.18
.
Suppose that
\Lambda
is path-connected, the conditions (a) and (c) in Theorem
1.19

and the following are satisfied:

(d)

There exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that

[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})-1]\cap[i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),i_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})-1]=\emptyset,

and either
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=1
or
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=1
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}

there exists a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}

and solutions
v_{k}\neq\bar{v}
of (
1.24
) with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that any two of these
v_{k}
are
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct and that

(v_{k})
converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=1
(resp.
\nu_{\tau,M}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=1
).

Theorem 1.21

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type and of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let
\tau>0
and
\Lambda
be a real interval, and let

H:\Lambda\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that each

H_{\lambda}:{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, is
C^{2}
and all its partial derivatives depend continuously on
the parameter
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Let
\bar{v}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}
be a nonconstant
\tau
-periodic solution of
\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,v(t))
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda
,
(therefore have the minimal period
\tau/p
with
p\in\mathbb{N}
),
and let
\gamma_{\lambda}(t)
be the fundamental matrix solution of
\dot{v}(t)=JH^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}(\bar{v}(t))v(t)
.
Suppose for some interior point
\mu
of
\Lambda
that

\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu}):=\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\mu}(\tau)-I_{2n})>1
,
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda}):=\dim{\rm Ker}(\gamma_{\lambda}(\tau)-I_{2n})=1

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and that

i_{\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda})
takes values
i_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})
and
i_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})+\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})-1

as
\lambda\in\Lambda
varies in two deleted half neighborhoods of
\mu
.
Then

(I)

The conclusions of Theorem
1.19
(after
\hat{\Lambda}
is replaced by
\Lambda
) hold.

(II)

If
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})>2
, one of the following alternatives occurs:

(II.1)

Equation (
1.24
) with
\lambda=\mu
has a sequence of
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions,
v_{k}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
(v_{k})
converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.

(II.2)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
there is a solution
v_{\lambda}
of
(
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda
, which is
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct with
\bar{v}
and
converges to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(II.3)

There is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{0}
of
\mu
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{0}\setminus\{\mu\}
, (
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda

has either infinitely many
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions
\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{v}
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
or at least two
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct solutions,
v_{\lambda}^{1}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{v}
and
v_{\lambda}^{2}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{v}
,
such that

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t),v^{1}_{\lambda}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,{v}_{\lambda}^{1}(t))\right]dt\neq\int^{\tau}_{0}\left[\frac{1}{2}(J\dot{v}^{2}_{\lambda}(t),v_{\lambda}^{2}(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+H(\lambda,{v}_{\lambda}^{2}(t))\right]dt;

and these
\bar{v}_{\lambda}^{k}
,
v^{1}_{\lambda}
and
v^{2}_{\lambda}
converge to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(III)

If
\nu_{\tau/p}(\gamma_{\mu})=1
, (which implies
p\geq 2
because
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})>1
), and
p
is equal to
2
(resp. a prime greater than
2
), then
one of (II.1) and the following (III.1) [resp. (III.2)] occurs:

(III.1)

There exist left and right neighborhoods
\Lambda^{-}
and
\Lambda^{+}
of
\mu
in
\Lambda

and integers
n^{+},n^{-}\geq 0
, such that
n^{+}+n^{-}\geq\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})
,
and for
\lambda\in\Lambda^{-}\setminus\{\mu\}
(resp.
\lambda\in\Lambda^{+}\setminus\{\mu\}
),
(
1.24
) with parameter value
\lambda
has at least
n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
)
{\mathbb{R}}
-distinct
solutions,
v_{\lambda}^{i}\notin\mathbb{R}\cdot\bar{v}
,
i=1,\cdots,n^{-}
(resp.
n^{+}
),
which
converge to
\bar{v}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
\lambda\to\mu
.

(III.2)

Replace
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})
with
\nu_{\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})/2
in (III.1).

1.3.
Bifurcations for brake orbits of periodic Hamiltonian systems

Assumption 1.22
.

For a real
\tau>0
and a topological space
\Lambda
,
let
H:\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a continuous function such that
each
H(\lambda,t,\cdot):{{\mathbb{R}}}^{2n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
(\lambda,t)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}
, is
C^{2}
,
and all its partial derivatives of
H
depend continuously on

(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
,
and that

\displaystyle H(\lambda,t+\tau,z)=H(\lambda,t,z)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n},

(1.35)

\displaystyle H(\lambda,-t,Nz)=H(\lambda,t,z)\quad\forall(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times{\mathbb{R}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2n},

(1.36)

where
N=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-I_{n}&0\\
0&I_{n}\end{array}\right)
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
let
v_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathbb{R}^{2n}
satisfy the following periodic Hamiltonian system

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,v(t)),\quad v(t+\tau)=v(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(-t)=Nv(t)\;\forall t\in{\mathbb{R}},

(1.37)

and
\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto v_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is continuous.

As pointed out below Assumption
1.1
it follows from Assumption
1.22
that

\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\ni(\lambda,t)\mapsto\dot{v}_{\lambda}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
is continuous.
When
H
is independent of
t
, solutions of (
1.37
) were called
brake orbits
.

We call
(\mu,v_{\mu})
with some
\mu\in\Lambda
a
bifurcation point
of (
1.37
)
if each neighborhood of it in
\Lambda\times W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
(or equivalently
\Lambda\times C^{1}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
)
contains elements
(\lambda,v)
, where
v\neq v_{\lambda}
is a solution of (
1.37
) for the parameter value
\lambda
.
Similarly, we can define a
bifurcation point along sequence
of (
1.37
) in the sense of Definition
C.2
.

Under Assumption
1.22
the linearized problem of (
1.37
) along
v_{\lambda}
is

\dot{v}(t)=J\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))v(t),\quad v(t+\tau)=v(t)\quad\hbox{and}\quad v(-t)=Nv(t)

(1.38)

for all
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Note that (
1.36
) implies

N^{T}\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,-t,Nz)=\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)\quad\hbox{and}\quad N^{T}\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,-t,Nz)N=\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,z).

Hence each

B_{\lambda}(t):=\nabla^{2}_{z}H(\lambda,t,v_{\lambda}(t))

satisfies Assumption
A.6
.
Let
\gamma_{\lambda}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\rm Sp}(2n,\mathbb{R})
be the fundamental matrix solution of

\dot{Z}(t)=JB_{\lambda}(t)Z(t)
. It has the Maslov-type index
(\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda}),\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda}))
defined by
(
A.13
) and (
A.14
).
For a solution of (
1.37
), since
NJ=-JN
and
N^{T}=N
, it is not hard to see that

Nu
does not necessarily satisfy (
1.37
) [even if
H
is independent of
t
].

Corresponding to Theorems
1.4
,
1.7
we have:

Theorem 1.23
.

Let Assumption
1.22
be satisfied.

(I)

(
Necessary condition
):

If
(\mu,v_{\lambda})
is a bifurcation point along sequences of
(
1.37
), that is,
there exists a sequence
(\lambda_{k})\subset\Lambda
converging to
\mu
and
solutions
v^{k}\neq v_{\lambda_{k}}
of (
1.37
) with
\lambda=\lambda_{k}

such that
v^{k}\to v_{\mu}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{0}
-topology as
k\to\infty
,
then
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
.

(II)

(
Sufficient condition
):

Let
\Lambda
be first countable.
Suppose for some
\mu\in\Lambda
that
there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\mu
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

[\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}),\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})+\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})]\cap[\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}),\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})+\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda_{k}^{-}})=0
.
Let
\hat{\Lambda}:=\{\mu,\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Then
(\mu,v_{\mu})
is a bifurcation point of (
1.37
)
with respect to the branch
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}\}

(and so
\{(\lambda,v_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}
).

(III)

(
Existence for bifurcations
):
Let
\Lambda
be path-connected. Suppose that
there exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that

[\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}}),\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})+\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})]\cap[\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}}),\mu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})+\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})]=\emptyset

and either
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
or
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
.
Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}

there exists a sequence
(t_{k})\subset[0,1]
converging to some
\bar{t}

and solutions
v^{k}\neq v_{\alpha(t_{k})}
of (
1.37
) with
\lambda=\alpha(t_{k})
,
k=1,2,\cdots
,
such that
(v^{k})
converges to
{v}_{\alpha(\bar{t})}
on any compact interval
I\subset{\mathbb{R}}
in
C^{1}
-topology as
k\to\infty
.
Moreover,
\alpha(\bar{t})
is not equal to
\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{-}
) if
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{+}})=0
(resp.
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda^{-}})=0
).

Theorem 1.24

(
Alternative bifurcations of Rabinowitz’s type and of Fadell-Rabinowitz’s type
)
.

Let Assumption
1.22
with
\Lambda
being a real interval be satisfied.
Suppose for some interior point
\mu
of
\Lambda
that

\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\mu})\neq 0
,
\nu_{1,\tau}(\gamma_{\lambda})=0

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\{\mu\}
near
\mu
, and
