, §6.1, Theorem 1]
)
Liu
[
35
, Theorem 2.1]
proved the following precise versions of (
A.27
) and (
A.26
) (in terms of the above notations)

\displaystyle j_{1,I_{2n}}(B|\ell I_{2n})=i_{1}(\Upsilon_{B})+n+2n\left[-\frac{\ell}{2\pi}\right],

(A.31)

\displaystyle\nu_{1,I_{2n}}(B|\ell I_{2n})=\nu_{1}(\Upsilon_{B}).

(A.32)

For
B\in L^{\infty}([0,\tau];\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2n}))
and
0\leq t\leq\tau
,
let

\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})=\dim{\rm Ker}(\Upsilon_{B}(t)-M)

and so
\nu_{0,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,0]})=\dim{\rm Ker}(I_{2n}-M)
.
As a generalization of
[
20
, Chapter I, §4, Theorem 6]

and
[
43
, Proposition 15.3]
, it was proved in
[
19
, Lemma 4.2]
that

i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{B})=i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})+\sum_{0\leq t<\tau}\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})

(A.33)

if
B
is positive definite, and

i_{\tau,M}(\Upsilon_{B})=i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n})-\sum_{0<t\leq\tau}\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})

(A.34)

if
B
is negative definite. Here
\xi_{2n}
is as in (
A.20
).

Remark A.4
.

The sum
\sum_{0<t\leq\tau}\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})
in (
A.34
)
was written as
\sum_{0<t<\tau}\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})
in
[
19
, (4.2)]
.
Actually, it was only asserted in
[
19
]
that
[
19
, (4.2)]
can be obtained
in a similar way to the proof of (
A.33
)
[
19
, (4.1)]
.
Since Proposition
A.1
implies that

i_{\tau,M}(\gamma)-i_{\tau,M}(\xi_{2n,\tau})=i^{M}_{\tau}(\gamma)-i^{M}_{\tau}(\xi_{2n,\tau})
, and

i_{\tau}^{M}(\xi_{2n,\tau})-i_{\tau}^{M}(\Upsilon_{B})=\sum_{0<t\leq\tau}\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{B}|_{[0,t]})

by
[
41
, (4.8)]
, (
A.34
) follows.

From (
A.33
) and (
A.34
) we derive:

Proposition A.5
.

Let
A\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{2n})
be either positive definite or negative definite,
and
\Upsilon_{\lambda A}(t)=\exp(\lambda tJA)
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
.

(
Clearly,
\Upsilon_{\lambda A}(t)=\Upsilon_{A}(\lambda t)
.
)
Then

\displaystyle\Gamma(A):=\{t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\,|\,\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)>0\}

is a discrete set in
\mathbb{R}
, and the following holds.

(
\lambda_{0}=0
and so
\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A}(t)=I_{2n}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
)

(i)

Let
A>0
. If
\Gamma(A)\cap(0,\infty)=\{\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{k}<\cdots<\lambda_{\kappa}\}
with
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
then

i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})&\hbox{$\lambda_{k-1}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{k}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{k}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{k+1}$ with $k\geq 0$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{\kappa}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{\kappa}A})&\hbox{if $\kappa\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lambda>\lambda_{\kappa}$};\end{array}\right.

(A.35)

and if
\Gamma(A)\cap(-\infty,0)=\{\lambda_{-\kappa}\cdots<\lambda_{-k}<\cdots<\lambda_{-1}\}
with

\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
then

i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{-k}<\lambda<\lambda_{-k+1}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{-k-1}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{-k}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-\kappa}A})&\hbox{if $\kappa\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lambda\leq\lambda_{-\kappa}$}.\end{array}\right.

(ii)

Let
A<0
. If
\Gamma(A)\cap(0,\infty)=\{\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{k}<\cdots<\lambda_{\kappa}\}

with
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, then

i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{k-1}<\lambda<\lambda_{k}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{k}\leq\lambda<\lambda_{k+1}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{\kappa}A})&\hbox{if $\kappa\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lambda\geq\lambda_{\kappa}$};\end{array}\right.

and if
\Gamma(A)\cap(-\infty,0)=\{\lambda_{-\kappa}\cdots<\lambda_{-k}<\cdots<\lambda_{-1}\}
with

\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
then

i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{-k}\leq\lambda<\lambda_{-k+1}$ with $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})&\hbox{if $\lambda_{-k-1}\leq\lambda<\lambda_{-k}$ and $k\geq 1$},\\
i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-\kappa}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-\kappa}A})&\hbox{if $\kappa\in\mathbb{N}$ and $\lambda<\lambda_{-\kappa}$}.\end{array}\right.

(A.36)

Proof.

By (
A.33
) and (
A.34
) we derive

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})-i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}&\sum_{0\leq t<\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)\quad\hbox{if $A>0$},\\
&-\sum_{0<t\leq\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)\quad\hbox{if $A<0$}\end{array}\right.

for
\lambda>0
, and

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})-i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}&\sum_{\lambda<t\leq 0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)\quad\hbox{if $A<0$},\\
&-\sum_{\lambda\leq t<0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)\quad\hbox{if $A>0$}\end{array}\right.

for
\lambda<0
. These imply that
\sum_{-N<t<N}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)<\infty
for any
N>0
.
The first claim is proved.

(i)
[
A>0
]. Let
\Gamma(A)\cap(0,\infty)=\{\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{k}<\cdots<\lambda_{\kappa}\}
with
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
Recall that
\nu_{t,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A}|_{[0,t]})=\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(t\lambda A)-M)
.
By (
A.33
) we have

i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})+\sum_{0\leq t<\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A}).

for
\lambda_{k-1}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{k}
with
k\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})+\sum_{0\leq t<\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})+\sum_{0\leq t<\lambda_{k}}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)+\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(\lambda_{k}JA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})

for
\lambda_{k}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{k+1}
with
k\geq 0
.
If
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, then the last two lines hold true for
k=\kappa
and
any
\lambda>\lambda_{\kappa}
.

Next, let
\Gamma(A)\cap(-\infty,0)=\{\lambda_{-\kappa}\cdots<\lambda_{-k}<\cdots<\lambda_{-1}\}
with
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
By (
A.34
),

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{\lambda\leq t<0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{\lambda_{-k}\leq t<0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})

for
\lambda_{-k}<\lambda<\lambda_{-k+1}
with
k\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{\lambda\leq t<0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{\lambda_{-k}\leq t<0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})

for
\lambda_{-k-1}<\lambda\leq\lambda_{-k}
with
k\geq 1
.
Clearly, when
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, the last two lines also hold true for
k=\kappa
and
any
\lambda\leq\lambda_{-\kappa}
.

(ii)
[
A<0
]. Assume that
\Gamma(A)\cap(0,\infty)=\{\lambda_{1}<\cdots<\lambda_{k}<\cdots<\lambda_{\kappa}\}
with
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.
By (
A.34
) we have

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{0<t\leq\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{0<t\leq\lambda_{k}}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})+\nu_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})

for
\lambda_{k-1}<\lambda<\lambda_{k}
with
k\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{0<t\leq\lambda}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})-\sum_{0<t\leq\lambda_{k}}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{k}A})

for
\lambda_{k}\leq\lambda<\lambda_{k+1}
with
k\geq 1
.
If

, the last two lines also hold true for

and
any

.

When
\Gamma(A)\cap(-\infty,0)=\{\lambda_{-\kappa}\cdots<\lambda_{-k}<\cdots<\lambda_{-1}\}
with

,

) we obtain

\displaystyle i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda A})=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{0}A})+\sum_{\lambda<t\leq 0}\dim{\rm Ker}(\exp(tJA)-M)=i_{1,M}(\Upsilon_{\lambda_{-k}A})

for

with

, and

for

with

.

and

.
∎

Let
N
be as in Assumption
1.22
, and let
\mu_{k,\tau}
and
\nu_{k,\tau}
,
k=1,2
, be as in (
A.13
) and (
A.14
).

Assumption A.6
.

(B1)

Let
B\in C({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n})

be a path of symmetric matrix which is
\tau
-periodic in time
t
,
i.e.,
B(t+\tau)=B(t)
for any
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

(B2)

N^{T}B(-t)N=B(t)
, that is, if
B(t)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}B_{11}(t)&B_{12}(t)\\
B_{21}(t)&B_{22}(t)\end{array}\right)
, then
B_{11},B_{22}:\;{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
are
even
at
t=0
and

\tau/2
, and
B_{12},B_{21}:\;{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
are

odd
at
t=0
and
\tau/2
.

Note that the operator
\Lambda_{I_{2n},\tau,0}
defined by (
2.3
) has the spectrum
\frac{2\pi}{\tau}\mathbb{Z}
.
Under the assumption (B1) we can choose
\kappa\notin\frac{2\pi}{\tau}\mathbb{Z}
such that

B(t)-\kappa I_{2n}\geq\varepsilon I_{2n}
for all
t
and small enough
\varepsilon>0
.

Consider the Hilbert subspaces of
L^{2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
and
W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
,

\displaystyle\textsf{L}_{\tau}=\left\{\,z\in L^{2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\,|\,z(-t)=Nz(t)\,{\rm a.e.}t\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\},

(A.39)

\displaystyle\textsf{W}_{\tau}=\left\{\,z\in W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\,|\,z(-t)=Nz(t)\,{\rm a.e.}\,t\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\}.

(A.40)

Then
\textsf{W}_{\tau}=W^{1,2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\cap\textsf{L}_{\tau}
.
Clearly, the operator
\Lambda_{I_{2n},\tau,0}
maps
\textsf{W}_{\tau}
into
\textsf{L}_{\tau}
.
Thus it restricts to a closed linear and self-adjoint operator on
\textsf{L}_{\tau}
with domain
\textsf{W}_{\tau}
,
denoted by
\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,0}
for clearness. Note that the spectrum of
\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,0}

is also
\frac{2\pi}{\tau}\mathbb{Z}
.
Choose
\kappa\notin\frac{2\pi}{\tau}\mathbb{Z}
as above. Then the operator

\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,\kappa I_{2n}}:\textsf{W}_{\tau}\subset\textsf{L}_{\tau}\to\textsf{L}_{\tau},\;u\mapsto\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,0}-\kappa u

is invertible and the inverse
(\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,\kappa I_{2n}})^{-1}:\textsf{L}_{\tau}\to\textsf{L}_{\tau}
is compact and self-adjoint.
It follows under the assumptions (B1) and (B2) that the quadratic form on
\textsf{L}_{\tau}
,

\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}}(u,v)=\frac{1}{2}\int^{\tau}_{0}\Bigl(((\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,\kappa I_{2n}})^{-1}u)(t),v(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+(C(t)u(t),v(t))_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\Bigr)\,dt

(A.41)

with
C(t)=(B(t)-\kappa I_{2n}I_{2n})^{-1}
,
is a Legendre form, and hence has finite Morse index
m^{-}(\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}})
and nullity
m^{0}(\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}})
.
Using
[
46
, Theorem 5.1]
and suitably modifying the proof of
[
35
, Theorem 2.1]

we can get the corresponding results with (
A.31
)-(
A.32
).

Theorem A.7
.

\displaystyle m^{-}(\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}})=\mu_{1,\tau}(\Upsilon_{B})-n\left[\frac{\kappa\tau}{2\pi}\right],

(A.42)

\displaystyle m^{0}(\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}})=\nu_{1,\tau}(\Upsilon_{B}),

(A.43)

If
u\in\textsf{L}_{\tau}
belongs to the zero space of
\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}}
, i.e.,

\textsf{Q}_{B,\kappa I_{2n}}(u,v)=0\;\forall v\in\textsf{L}_{\tau}
, it easily follows that

w:=(\tilde{\Lambda}_{I_{2n},\tau,\kappa I_{2n}})^{-1}u\in\textsf{W}_{\tau}
satisfies
J\dot{w}(t)+B(t)w(t)=0

for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Thus
u\neq 0
implies that
w(t)\neq 0
for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
Repeating the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 3.18]
we may obtain:

Theorem A.8
.

Let
B_{1},B_{2}\in C({\mathbb{R}},{\mathbb{R}}^{2n\times 2n})
satisfy Assumption
A.6
. Assume
B_{2}>B_{1}
. Then
for each
\kappa\notin\frac{2\pi}{\tau}\mathbb{Z}
such that

B_{i}(t)-\kappa I_{2n}\geq\varepsilon I_{2n}
for all
t
and small enough
\varepsilon>0
,
i=1,2
, it holds that

\displaystyle m^{-}(\textsf{Q}_{B_{2},\kappa I_{2n}})\geq m^{-}(\textsf{Q}_{B_{1},\kappa I_{2n}})+m^{0}(\textsf{Q}_{B_{1},\kappa I_{2n}})

or equivalently
\mu_{1,\tau}(\Upsilon_{B_{2}})\geq\mu_{1,\tau}(\Upsilon_{B_{1}})+\nu_{1,\tau}(\Upsilon_{B_{1}})
.

Using the last two theorems it is direct to give corresponding results with

[
35
, §4]
and
[
19
, §5]
for the system (
1.37
) under suitable
conditions on
H
.
They will be provided elsewhere.

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
1.3

Since the operator
\Lambda_{M,\tau}\equiv\Lambda_{M,\tau,0}
defined by (
2.3
)
is a self-adjoint unbounded linear operator in
L^{2}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{2n})
with domain

{\rm dom}(\Lambda_{M,\tau})=W^{1,2}_{M}([0,\tau];\mathbb{R}^{2n})
,
by
[
31
, Remarks 2.2,2.3]
, all eigenvalues of
\Lambda_{M,\tau}
have form

\cdots\leq\lambda_{-k}\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{-1}<0<\lambda_{1}\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{k}\leq\cdots

and there exists a unit orthogonal basis of
L^{2}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{2n})
,

\{e_{j}\,|\,\pm j\in\mathbb{N}\}\cup\{e_{0}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{q}
,
such that
|e_{j}(t)|=|e_{0}^{i}(t)|\equiv 1\;\forall t
,
{\rm Ker}(\Lambda_{M,\tau})={\rm Span}(\{e_{0}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{q})
and that each
e_{j}

is an eigenvector corresponding to
\lambda_{j}
,
j=\pm 1,\pm 2,\cdots
.

For
s\geq 0
we say that
x\in L^{2}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{2n})
belongs to
E^{s}_{M}
if and only if

x=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}x_{k}e_{k}
satisfies

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|\lambda_{k}|^{2s}x_{k}^{2}<\infty.

It is easy to prove that
E^{s}_{M}
is a Hilbert space with respect to the inner product

\langle x,y\rangle_{E^{s}_{M}}=\langle x_{0},y_{0}\rangle_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}+\sum_{k\neq 0}|\lambda_{k}|^{2s}x_{k}y_{k},\quad x,y\in E^{s}_{M}.

Denote the associated norm by
\|\cdot\|_{E^{s}_{M}}
.
Note that
E^{0}_{M}=L^{2}([0,\tau],\mathbb{R}^{2n})
and
\|\cdot\|_{E^{0}_{M}}=\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}}
.
For
t>s\geq 0
, the inclusion
E^{t}_{M}\hookrightarrow E^{s}_{M}
is compact (
[
31
, Proposition 2.5]
).

Proof of Proposition
1.3
.

Step 1
(
Prove
(i)).
Let
(\lambda_{k},\bar{u}_{k})
be a sequence of solutions of the problem
(
1.3
) converging to
(\mu,u_{\mu})
in
\Lambda\times C^{0}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.
It suffices to prove that

|\dot{\bar{u}}_{k}(t)-\dot{u}_{\mu}(t)|=|J\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,\bar{u}_{k}(t))-J\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,u_{\mu}(t))|=|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,\bar{u}_{k}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,u_{\mu}(t))|

uniformly converges to zero on
[0,\tau]
. Otherwise, there exists
\varepsilon>0
and subsequences

(k_{i})
and
(t_{i})\subset[0,1]
such that

|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k_{i}},t_{i},\bar{u}_{k_{i}}(t_{i}))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t_{i},u_{\mu}(t_{i}))|\geq\varepsilon,\quad\forall i=1,2,\cdots.

(B.1)

Passing to a subsequence (if necessary) we can assume
t_{i}\to t_{0}\in[0,1]
. Note that

\displaystyle|\bar{u}_{k_{i}}(t_{i})-u_{\mu}(t_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\bar{u}_{k_{i}}(t_{i})-u_{\mu}(t_{i})|+|u_{\mu}(t_{i})-u_{\mu}(t_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\bar{u}_{k_{i}}-u_{\mu}\|_{C^{0}}+|u_{\mu}(t_{i})-u_{\mu}(t_{0})|\to 0.

Since
\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,t,z)
is continuous in
(\lambda,t,z)\in\Lambda\times[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{2n}
by Assumption
1.1
,
letting
i\to\infty
in (
B.1
) we obtain

0=|\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t_{0},u_{\mu}(t_{0}))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t_{0},u_{\mu}(t_{0}))|\geq\varepsilon
, which is a contradiction.

Step 2
(
Prove
(ii)).
Let
(\lambda_{k},\bar{u}_{k})
be a sequence of solutions of the problem
(
1.3
) converging to
(\mu,u_{\mu})
in
\Lambda\times\mathbb{E}
.
By
[
31
, Proposition 2.10]
, all
\bar{u}_{k}
and
u_{\mu}
are
C^{1}
.

Firstly, we assume
M\neq I_{2n}
. By
[
31
, Proposition 2.5]
,
we have a compact inclusion
\mathbb{E}\to L^{2}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
and therefore

\|{\bar{u}}_{k}-{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{r}}\to 0

(B.2)

because
1<r\leq 2
. Note that

\displaystyle\|\dot{\bar{u}}_{k}-\dot{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,\bar{u}_{k}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,u_{\mu}(t))|dt

(B.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int^{\tau}_{0}|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,{u}_{\mu}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,u_{\mu}(t))|dt

\displaystyle+\int^{\tau}_{0}|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,\bar{u}_{k}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,u_{\mu}(t))|dt.

Since
\nabla_{z}H
is continuous, by (
1.10
)
it follows from Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int^{\tau}_{0}|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,{u}_{\mu}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\mu,t,u_{\mu}(t))|dt=0.

(B.4)

Note that
[
51
, Proposition C.1]
is still true for
p\geq 1
and that
\Lambda_{0}:=\{\mu,\lambda_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}

is sequential compact. From (
1.10
) we deduce that
maps

L^{r}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})\to L^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n}),\;u\mapsto\nabla_{z}H(\lambda,\cdot;u(\cdot))

are uniform continuous at
{u}_{\mu}
with respect to
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
. This result and (
B.2
) imply

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int^{\tau}_{0}|\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,\bar{u}_{k}(t))-\nabla_{z}H(\lambda_{k},t,u_{\mu}(t))|dt=0.

It follows from this, (
B.4
) and (
B.3
) that

\|\dot{\bar{u}}_{k}-\dot{u}_{\mu}\|_{L^{1}}\to 0
and so
\|{\bar{u}}_{k}-{u}_{\mu}\|_{1,1}\to 0

by (
B.2
). Then

\|{\bar{u}}_{k}-{u}_{\mu}\|_{C^{0}}\leq\max\{\tau^{-1},1\}\|{\bar{u}}_{k}-{u}_{\mu}\|_{1,1}\to 0.

(B.5)

By the proof in Step 1,
\bar{u}_{k}\to u_{\mu}
in
C^{1}([0,\tau];{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
.

Next, we consider the case
M=I_{2n}
. Then there exists a stronger result than
[
31
, Proposition 2.5]
:

\mathbb{E}=H^{1/2}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
is compactly embedded in
L^{s}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
for each
s\in[1,\infty)

(cf.
[
61
, Proposition 6.6]
or Theorem 3 in
[
29
, Appendix A.3]
). Therefore
(
B.2
) is still true for the present
r
. Repeating the above arguments we may obtain the desired conclusions.
∎

Question B.1
.

When
M\neq I_{2n}
, can
\mathbb{E}
be embedded in
L^{s}(S_{\tau};{\mathbb{R}}^{2n})
for each
s\in(2,\infty)
?
If this is affirmative, then the condition “
1<r\leq 2
” may be replaced by “
1<r<\infty
”.

Appendix C
Generalizations and corrections for related results in
[
48
,
50
,
52
]

We begin with the following remark about
[
48
, Theorems A.1,A.2]
.

Remark C.1
.

In
[
48
, Theorems A.1,A.2]
we assumed that the topology space
\Lambda
is compact.
Actually, “compact” can be replaced by “first countable and sequential compact”.
Here we say the topology space
\Lambda
to be
sequential compact
if every
sequence
(\lambda_{n})
of points in it has a subsequence converging to some
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
Recall that compact topology spaces satisfying the first axiom of countability must be sequential compact.
In what follows let us point out how these different conditions of compactness are used in the proofs of
[
48
, Theorems A.1,A.2]
.

(i)

For the proof of “
\phi
is continuous” in
[
48
, line 20, page 2980]
using nets we can complete it without any requirements.
If
\Lambda
is first countable we can easily prove this claim with sequences. (See (v) below.)

(ii)

For the proof of “Step 1” in
[
48
, page 2980]
, from the proof of Claim A.3 in the proof of
[
48
, Theorem A.2]

we see that the sequential compactness of
\Lambda
was actually used. When
\Lambda
is only compact we may work with nets instead of sequences
to complete the desired proof.

(iii)

Our proof of “Step 2” in
[
48
, pages 2980-2981]
actually used the assumption that

\Lambda
is first countable and sequential compact. If
\Lambda
is only compact the expected proof can be completed with nets.

(iv)

The proof of “(A.2)” in
[
48
, page 2981]
actually used the sequential compactness of

\Lambda
. For compact
\Lambda
a complete proof can be given with nets.

(v)

As in (i), for the proof of “
\phi
is continuous” in Step 7 in
[
48
, page 2983]

we need to use nets. It suffices to use sequences for first countable
\Lambda
.

These five remarks are effective for the proof of
[
48
, Theorem A.2]
.

In summary, the topology space
\Lambda
in
[
48
, Theorems A.1,A.2]
should be either
compact
or
first countable and sequential compact
.
Because of these we have also:

(vi)

The topology space
\Lambda
in
[
50
, Theorem A.3]
and
[
52
, Theorem 3.3]

should be assumed to be
first countable and sequential compact
because first countable compact spaces are sequential compact
and our proof for (A.11) in the proof of
[
50
, Theorem A.3]
actually used the fact that
\Lambda
has a countable neighborhood basis at
\lambda^{\ast}
.

Let
\Lambda
be a topological space, and let
X,Y
be Banach spaces.
For continuous maps
F:\Lambda\times X\to Y
and
\Lambda\ni\lambda\mapsto x_{\lambda}\in X

satisfying
F(\lambda,x_{\lambda})=0
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
, recall that

(\lambda^{\ast},x_{\lambda^{\ast}})\in\Lambda\times X
is called a
bifurcation point
of

F(\lambda,x)=0
in
\Lambda\times X
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,x_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if every neighborhood
U
of
(\lambda^{\ast},x_{\lambda^{\ast}})
contains a point
(\lambda,y_{\lambda})\neq(\lambda,x_{\lambda})

satisfying
F(\lambda,y_{\lambda})=0
. If
\Lambda
is first countable,
this definition is equivalent to the following:

Definition C.2
.

Under the assumptions above,
(\lambda^{\ast},x_{\lambda^{\ast}})\in\Lambda\times X
is said to be
a
bifurcation point along sequences
of

F(\lambda,x)=0
in
\Lambda\times X
with respect to the trivial branch
\{(\lambda,x_{\lambda})\,|\,\lambda\in\Lambda\}

if there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{n},y_{n})\}_{n\geq 1}
of solutions of
F(\lambda,x)=0
in
\Lambda\times X
converging to
(\lambda^{\ast},x_{\lambda^{\ast}})

in
\Lambda\times X
such that
y_{n}\neq x_{\lambda_{n}}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Clearly, a bifurcation point along sequences must be a bifurcation point.

Hypothesis C.3

(
[
50
, Hypothesis 1.3]
)
.

Let
H
be a Hilbert space with inner product
(\cdot,\cdot)_{H}

and the induced norm
\|\cdot\|
, and let
X
be a Banach space with
norm
\|\cdot\|_{X}
, such that
X\subset H
is dense in
H
and
\|x\|\leq\|x\|_{X}\;\forall x\in X
.
For an open neighborhood
U
of
0
in
H
,
U\cap X

is also an open neighborhood of
0
in
X
, denoted by
U^{X}
.
Let
\mathcal{L}:U\to\mathbb{R}
be a continuous functional satisfying the following
conditions:

(F1)

\mathcal{L}
is continuously directional
differentiable and
D\mathcal{L}(0)=0
.

(F2)

There exists a continuous and continuously directional differentiable
map
A:U^{X}\to X
, which is also strictly Fréchet differentiable
at
0
, such that

D\mathcal{L}(x)[u]=(A(x),u)_{H}
for all
x\in U\cap X
and
u\in X
.

(F3)

There exists a map
B:U\cap X\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
such that

(DA(x)[u],v)_{H}=(B(x)u,v)_{H}
for all
x\in U\cap X
and

u,v\in X
. (So
B(x)
induces an element in
\mathscr{L}(X)
, denoted by
B(x)|_{X}
,
and
B(x)|_{X}=DA(x)\in\mathscr{L}(X),\;\forall x\in U\cap X
.)

(C)

\{u\in H\,|\,B(0)(u)\in X\}\subset X
,
in particular
{\rm Ker}(B(0))\subset X
.

(D)

B
satisfies the same conditions as in
[
50
, Hypothesis 1.1]
, that is,

(D1)

\{u\in H\,|\,B(0)u=\mu u,\;\mu\leq 0\}\subset X
,

and
B
has a decomposition
B=P+Q
, where for each
x\in U\cap X
,
P(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is positive definitive and

Q(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact, such that the maps
P
and
Q
also satisfy the following
properties:

(D2)

For any sequence
(x_{k})\subset U\cap X
with
\|x_{k}\|\to 0
, it holds that
\|P(x_{k})u-P(0)u\|\to 0
for any
u\in H
.

(D3)

The map
Q:U\cap X\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is continuous at
0
with respect to the topology
on
H
.

(D4)

For any sequence
(x_{k})\subset U\cap X
with
\|x_{k}\|\to 0
, there exist
constants
C_{0}>0
and
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

(P(x_{k})u,u)_{H}\geq C_{0}\|u\|^{2}
for all
u\in H
and for all
k\geq k_{0}
.

We say an isolated critical point
p
of a
C^{1}
-functional
f
on a Banach manifold
\mathcal{M}

to be
homological visible
if there exists a nonzero critical group
C_{m}(f,p;\mathbf{K})
for some
Abel group
\mathbf{K}
.

In view of Remark
C.1
we may revise and refine
[
52
, Theorem 3.5]
as follows.

Theorem C.4
.

Let
H
,
X
and
U
be as in Hypothesis
C.3
,
and let
\Lambda
be the first countable topology space.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\in C^{1}(U,\mathbb{R})
,
\lambda\in\Lambda
, be a continuous family of functionals
satisfying
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\lambda}(0)=0
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda
.
For each
\lambda\in\Lambda
, assume that
there exist maps
A_{\lambda}\in C^{1}(U^{X},X)
and
B_{\lambda}:U\cap X\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)

such that

a)

\Lambda\times U^{X}\ni(\lambda,x)\to A_{\lambda}(x)\in X
is continuous;

b)

for all
x\in U\cap X
and
u,v\in X
,

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)[u]=(A_{\lambda}(x),u)_{H}\quad\hbox{and}\quad(DA_{\lambda}(x)[u],v)_{H}=(B_{\lambda}(x)u,v)_{H};

(C.1)

c)

B_{\lambda}
has a decomposition

B_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}+Q_{\lambda}
, where for each
x\in U\cap X
,

P_{\lambda}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is positive definitive and

Q_{\lambda}(x)\in\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is compact.

Suppose also for some
\lambda^{\ast}\in\Lambda
that
P_{\lambda}
and
Q_{\lambda}
satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

For each
h\in H
, it holds that
\|P_{\lambda}(x)h-P_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)h\|\to 0

as
x\in U\cap X
approaches to
0
in
H
and
\lambda\in\Lambda
converges to
\lambda^{\ast}
.

(ii)

For some small
\delta>0
, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

(P_{\lambda}(x)u,u)\geq c_{0}\|u\|^{2}\quad\forall u\in H,\;\forall x\in\bar{B}_{H}(0,\delta)\cap X,\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda.

(iii)

Q_{\lambda}:U\cap X\to\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
is uniformly continuous at
0
with respect to
\lambda\in\Lambda
.

(iv)

If
\lambda\in\Lambda
converges to
\lambda^{\ast}
then

\|Q_{\lambda}(0)-Q_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\|\to 0
.

(v)

Each tube
(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},H,X,U,A_{\lambda},B_{\lambda}=P_{\lambda}+Q_{\lambda})
,

\lambda\in\Lambda
, satisfies Hypothesis
C.3
.

Then there holds:

(A)

If
(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0

in
\Lambda\times U
, (which implies that
0
is an isolated critical point
of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
for each
\lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
),

\Lambda
is sequential compact,
and
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))\neq\{0\}
, then

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}},0;{\bf K})
for any

\lambda
in a small neighborhood of
\lambda^{\ast}\in\Lambda
and for any Abel group
{\bf K}
.
(
Note
: From the proof it is easily seen that
(v) need only to be satisfied for
\lambda^{\ast}
.)
In addition, the condition “
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}},0;{\bf K})
”
may be changed into “
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
”
provided for each
\lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}\in C^{2}(U^{X},\mathbb{R})

and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous as a map from
U^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
.

(B)

Suppose that there exist two sequences in
\Lambda
converging to
\lambda^{\ast}
,
(\lambda_{k}^{-})
and

(\lambda_{k}^{+})
, such that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
[\mu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda^{-}_{k}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}]=\emptyset
and one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(B.1)

There exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
such that
0
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
.

(B.2)

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}\in C^{2}(U^{X},\mathbb{R})
and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous
as a map from
U^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
for each
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
, and there exists

\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
such that
0
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}
.

(B.3)

Either
\nu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}=0
or
\nu_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}=0
.

(Here
\mu_{\lambda}=\dim H^{-}_{\lambda}
and
\nu_{\lambda}=\dim H^{0}_{\lambda}
are dimensions of
the negative definite and zero spaces
H^{-}_{\lambda}
and
H^{0}_{\lambda}
of
B_{\lambda}(0)
, respectively.)
Then
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
and there exists a sequence
\{(\lambda_{k},x_{k})\}_{k\geq 1}
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times U

converging to
(\lambda^{\ast},0)
, where
\hat{\Lambda}:=\{\lambda^{\ast},\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{N}\}
, such that each
x_{k}

is a nonzero solution of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{k}}(x)=0
,
k=1,2,\cdots
.
In particular,

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0

in
\hat{\Lambda}\times U
(and so in
\Lambda\times U
).

That
(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0

in
\Lambda\times U
also implies that
(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not a bifurcation point of
D(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}})(x)=0

in
\Lambda\times U^{X}
. The latter claim shows that

0
is also an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}
for each
\lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}

and thus
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
is well-defined.
The condition “
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
”
is easily checked in applications.

Proof.

Step 1
[
Prove (A)
].
Since
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))\neq\{0\}
and
\Lambda
is first countable and sequential compact,
by Remark
C.1
(vi) we may use
[
52
, Theorem 3.3]
or
[
52
, Theorem A.3]
to find a neighborhood

\Lambda_{0}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\Lambda
,
\epsilon>0
, a (unique)
C^{0}
map

\psi:\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to X^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

which is
C^{1}
in the second variable and
satisfies
\psi(\lambda,0)=0\;\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
and

P^{\pm}_{\lambda^{\ast}}A_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z))=0\quad\forall(\lambda,z)\in\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon),

(C.2)

and a homeomorphism
\Phi_{\lambda}
from
B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
onto
an open neighborhood of
0
in
H
satisfying
\Phi_{\lambda}(0)=0
for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,
such that for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\circ\Phi_{\lambda}(z,u^{+}+u^{-})=\|u^{+}\|^{2}-\|u^{-}\|^{2}+\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}}(z+\psi({\lambda},z))

(C.3)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\forall(z,u^{+}+u^{-})\in B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times\left(B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\right).

Moreover, the functional

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}:B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\to\mathbb{R},\;z\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(z+\psi({\lambda},z))

(C.4)

is of class
C^{2}
, and

\displaystyle d\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}(z)[\zeta]=\bigl(A_{\lambda}(z+\psi(\lambda,z)),\zeta\bigr)_{H},\quad\forall(z,\zeta)\in B_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}.

(C.5)

By (
C.2
) and (
C.5
), for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
,
the map
z\mapsto z+\psi({\lambda},z))
induces an one-to-one correspondence
between the critical points of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}
near
0\in H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

and zeros of
A_{\lambda}
near
0\in X
.

Since
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U

is not a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
\Lambda\times U
,
by shrinking
\Lambda_{0}
towards
\lambda^{\ast}
(in
\Lambda
) we can find
\delta>0
such that

\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
has only trivial solutions in
\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H}(0,\delta)\subset\Lambda_{0}\times U

(and therefore
A_{\lambda}(x)=0
has only trivial solutions in
\Lambda_{0}\times B_{X}(0,\delta)\subset\Lambda_{0}\times B_{H}(0,\delta)
).
It follows from this and the sentence below (
C.5
) that after shrinking
\epsilon>0
(if necessary),

for each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
the functional
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
has a unique
critical point
0
in
{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
.

(C.6)

Since
\psi
is continuous, by the assumption c), (
C.4
) and (
C.5
), maps

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}\times{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\ni(\lambda,z)\mapsto\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(z)\in{\mathbb{R}}\quad\hbox{and}

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}\times{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\ni(\lambda,z)\mapsto d\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}(z)\in H_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}=X_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{0}

are continuous. We conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}}\sup\{|\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(z)-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ}(z)|:\,z\in\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon/2)\}=0,

(C.7)

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}}\sup\{\|d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(z)-d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ}(z)\|:\,z\in\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon/2)\}=0.

(C.8)

Clearly, (
C.8
) implies (
C.7
) because
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(0)-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ}(0)=\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(0)-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0)\to 0
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.
Note that
\Lambda
has a countable neighborhood basis at
\lambda^{\ast}
. By contradiction (
C.8
) may follow
from the sequential compactness of
\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon/2)
.
For any two different points in
\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon/2)
,
z_{1},z_{2}
, it follows from the mean value theorem that

\displaystyle\frac{|(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ})(z_{1})-(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ})(z_{2})|}{|z_{1}-z_{2}|}\leq\int^{1}_{0}\|d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}(tz_{1}+(1-t)z_{2})-d\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ}(tz_{1}+(1-t)z_{2})\|dt.

This and (
C.8
) lead to

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ}\|_{\infty}:=\sup\left\{\frac{|(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ})(z_{1})-(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\circ}-\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}^{\circ})(z_{2})|}{|z_{1}-z_{2}|}\,\Bigg|\,z_{1},z_{2}\in\bar{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon/2),\;z_{1}\neq z_{2}\right\}\to 0

as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
. By this and (
C.6
)-(
C.7
) we derive from
[
18
, Theorem 5.1]
that
critical groups

\displaystyle C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda^{\ast}},0;{\bf K})\quad\forall\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}

(C.9)

for any Abel group
{\bf K}
by shrinking
\Lambda_{0}
towards
\lambda^{\ast}
(if necessary).
Moreover
0\in U
is a unique critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
in
B_{H}(0,\delta)\subset U
.
Using (
C.6
) and
[
50
, Corollary A.5]
we obtain

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}},0;{\bf K})=C_{q-\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{{\lambda}},0;{\bf K}),\quad\forall(\lambda,q)\in\Lambda_{0}\times(\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}).

This and (
C.9
) lead to

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}},0;{\bf K})
for all
\lambda\in\Lambda_{0}
.

Under the assumptions in part “In addition” of (A), if
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda}(0))\neq\{0\}
(resp.
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda}(0))=\{0\}
)
by Jiang
[
32
]
there exists a splitting lemma (resp. Morse lemma) for
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}

near
0\in U^{X}
. (The case of
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda}(0))=\{0\}
can be easily obtained from the proof therein.)
By
[
32
, Corollary 2.8]
we get
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})
for each
\lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
.
The required conclusion follows from the last one.

Step 2
[
Prove (B)
]. Firstly, we prove
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
, i.e.,
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda^{\ast}}(0))\neq\{0\}
. Otherwise,
in the proof of
[
52
, Theorem A.3]
, replacing
\Lambda
by the first countable and sequential compact space

\hat{\Lambda}
we need not use the implicit function theorem and obtain that the lines 3-4 on the page 1294 of
[
50
]
become

\displaystyle\big(D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u^{+}+u^{-}_{2})-D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u^{+}+u^{-}_{1})\big)[u^{-}_{2}-u^{-}_{1}]\leq-\mathfrak{a}_{1}\|u^{-}_{2}-u^{-}_{1}\|^{2},

\displaystyle D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u^{+}+u^{-})[u^{+}-u^{-}]\geq\mathfrak{a}_{2}(\|u^{+}\|^{2}+\|u^{-}\|^{2})

(C.10)

for all
(\lambda,u^{+},u^{-})\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
,
where
\mathfrak{a}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{a}_{2}
are positive constants and
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
is a neighborhood of
\lambda^{\ast}
in
\hat{\Lambda}
.
In particular, the second inequality implies that

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u^{+})[u^{+}]\geq\mathfrak{a}_{2}(\|u^{+}\|^{2})

for all
(\lambda,u^{+})\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
.
(Actually, since
H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\subset X
and
H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\cap X
is dense in
H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
, from the second inequality we easily derive
that
(B_{\lambda}(0)u^{+},u^{+})_{H}\geq\mathfrak{a}_{2}\|u^{+}\|^{2}
and
(B_{\lambda}(0)u^{-},u^{-})_{H}\leq-\mathfrak{a}_{2}\|u^{-}\|^{2}

for all
(\lambda,u^{+},u^{-})\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}\times H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
.)
By Remark
C.1
and
[
48
, Theorem A.1]
there exists
\epsilon>0
, an open neighborhood

U
of
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times\{0\}
in
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times H
and a homeomorphism

\phi:\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times\bigl(B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\bigr)\to U

with
\phi(\lambda,0)=(\lambda,0)
such that
for all
(\lambda,u^{+},u^{-})\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\times B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\phi(\lambda,u^{+}+u^{-}))=\|u^{+}\|^{2}-\|u^{-}\|^{2}.

(C.11)

Note by (
C
) that
0
is a unique critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
in

B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)\oplus B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)

for each
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
, and that
each
\phi(\lambda,\cdot)
is a homeomorphism
from
B_{H^{+}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)+B_{H^{-}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
onto an open neighborhood of
0
in
H
.
It follows from these and (
C.11
) that for any Abel group
{\bf K}
,

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=\delta^{q}_{\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}}{\bf K}\quad\forall(\lambda,q)\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}\times\mathbb{Z},

(C.12)

where
\delta^{q}_{p}=1
if
p=q
, and
\delta^{q}_{p}=0
if
p\neq q
.

Choose a large
k
so that
\lambda_{k}^{+}
and
\lambda_{k}^{-}
belong to
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
.

Then
0
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
in

U
for each large
k
.

Because of the assumption (v) in Theorem
C.4
it follows from
[
48
, (2.7)]
and the shifting theorem
[
48
, Corollary 2.6]
that
for any Abel group
{\bf K}
and
\star=+,-
,

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}},0;{\bf K})=\delta^{q}_{\mu_{\lambda^{\star}_{k}}}{\bf K}\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}\;\hbox{if}\;\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}=0,

(C.13)

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}},0;{\bf K})=0\;\;\forall q\notin[\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}},\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}+\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}]\;\hbox{if}\;\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}>0,

(C.14)

respectively. (See
[
13
, Corollary 5.1]
for the latter). From (
C.12
), (
C.13
) and
(
C.14
) we deduce

\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}\in[\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}},\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}],

which contradicts the first condition in (B). Hence
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
.

By contradiction, suppose that
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\hat{\Lambda}\times U

is not a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
\hat{\Lambda}\times U
.

We can shrink
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
towards
\lambda^{\ast}
(in
\hat{\Lambda}
) and
\epsilon>0

so that for each
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
,

(1)

the functional
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
has an isolated critical point
0
in
U
,

(2)

the functional
\mathcal{L}^{\circ}_{\lambda}
has a unique
critical point
0
in
{B}_{H^{0}_{\lambda^{\ast}}}(0,\epsilon)
.

Since
\hat{\Lambda}
is first countable and sequential compact,
the first paragraph in Step 1 is still valid
after
\Lambda
and
\Lambda_{0}
are replaced by
\hat{\Lambda}
and
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
, respectively.
Repeating the arguments in the second paragraph in Step 1, for any Abel group
{\bf K}
we get

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}},0;{\bf K})

for all
\lambda\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
, and hence

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}},0;{\bf K})=C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}},0;{\bf K}),\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}

(C.15)

if
k
is so large that
\lambda_{k}^{+},\lambda_{k}^{-}\in\hat{\Lambda}_{0}
.
For such a large
k
, we can obtain a contradiction under any of the assumptions (B.1), (B.2) and (B.3) as follows.

Case (B.1)
: Because of (1),
0
is a homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}}
.
Combing with (
C.15
) we derive

C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}},0;{\bf K})=C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0

for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.
As above this and (
C.13
)-(
C.14
) yield
a contradiction to the first condition in (B).

Case (B.2)
:
Since (1) implies that
0\in U^{X}
is an isolated critical point
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}
for any
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}\}
,

0
is a homological visible critical point of either

\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}}|_{U^{X}}
or
\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}}|_{U^{X}}
.
it follows from our assumptions that
Then either
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0

for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
or
C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})\neq 0
for some Abel group
{\bf K}^{\prime}
and some
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.
As in the final paragraph in Step 1, for any Abel group
{\bf G}
we have also
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf G})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf G})
for
\lambda=\lambda^{+}_{k},\lambda^{-}_{k}
.
These and (
C.15
) lead to either

\displaystyle C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}},0;{\bf K})=C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0\quad\hbox{or}\quad C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{+}}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})=C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda_{k}^{-}}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})\neq 0.

As above, from these and (
C.13
)-(
C.14
) we derive
a contradiction to the first condition in (B).

Case (B.3)
: This case can also be divided into three cases.

Case 1
:
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}=0
and
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}=0
. Because of (1), we may use (
C.13
) and
(
C.15
) to derive

\mu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}=\mu_{\lambda^{\ast}}=\mu_{\lambda^{-}_{k}}
, which contradicts
the first condition in (B).

Case 2
:
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}=0
and
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}>0
. Because (1),
(
C.13
) with
\star=+
, (
C.15
) and
(
C.14
) with
\star=-
show that
\mu_{\lambda^{+}_{k}}\in[\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}},\mu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}]
.
This contradicts the first condition in (B).

Case 3
:
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{+}}>0
and
\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{-}}=0
. This case may be proved as in Case 2.
∎

The following bifurcation theorem may be viewed as a global version of Theorem
C.4
.

Theorem C.5
.

Under the assumptions a)-c) and (v) of Theorem
C.4

without requirement of the first countability for

\Lambda
, suppose that the topology space
\Lambda
is path connected and the following conditions are satisfied.

d)

P_{\lambda}
and
Q_{\lambda}
satisfy the conditions
(i)-(iv) of Theorem
C.4
for any
\lambda^{\ast}\in\Lambda
;

e)

There exist two points
\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\in\Lambda
such that
one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(e.1)

Either
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
,
or
0
is not an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
or
0
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}
and

C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})
are not isomorphic for some Abel group
{\bf K}

and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, “
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}},0;{\bf K})
”
in the third case may be changed into “
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
and
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
” provided also that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}\in C^{2}(U^{X},\mathbb{R})

and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous as a map from
U^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)

for each
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
.

(e.2)

[\mu_{\lambda^{+}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda^{-}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}}]=\emptyset
,
and there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that
0
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
.

(e.3)

[\mu_{\lambda^{+}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda^{-}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}}]=\emptyset
,
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}\in C^{2}(U^{X},\mathbb{R})
and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous
as a map from
U^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)
for each
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
, and
there exists
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
such that

0
is an either nonisolated or homological visible critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}
.

(e.4)

[\mu_{\lambda^{+}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda^{-}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}}]=\emptyset
,
and either
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=0
or
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=0
.

Then for any path
\alpha:[0,1]\to\Lambda
connecting
\lambda^{+}
to
\lambda^{-}
there exists
\bar{t}\in[0,1]

such that
(\bar{t},0)\in[0,1]\times U
is a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\alpha(t)}(x)=0
in
[0,1]\times U
,
and therefore that
(\alpha(\bar{t}),0)\in\Lambda\times U
is a bifurcation point along sequences of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
\Lambda\times U
.

Moreover,
if as a map from
U\cap X
to
H
,
A_{\lambda^{+}}
(resp.
A_{\lambda^{-}}
)
has a Gâteaux derivative
B_{\lambda^{+}}(x)
(resp.
B_{\lambda^{-}}(x)
) at every point
x\in U\cap X

and
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=0
(resp.
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=0
), then
\alpha(\bar{t})\neq\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\alpha(\bar{t})\neq\lambda^{-}
).

In e.1), even if
0
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}

it is possible that either
(\lambda^{+},0)
or
(\lambda^{+},0)
is a bifurcation point.
The second part in e.1) and e.4) are, sometimes, more conveniently used in applications, see
[
54
]
.

Proof.

Clearly, it suffices to prove the case that
\Lambda=[0,1]
and
\lambda^{+}=0,\lambda^{-}=1
.

By contradiction, suppose that
[0,1]\times U

contains no bifurcations point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
[0,1]\times U
.

Then there exists a small
\varepsilon>0
such that

for each
\lambda\in[0,1]
,
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
has a unique
solution
0
in
{B}_{H}(0,\varepsilon)\subset U
.

(C.16)

(In particular, this implies that
0
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
for each
\lambda\in[0,1]
.)
We conclude that for any Abel group
{\bf K}
and any
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda}},0;{\bf K})=C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{\prime}}},0;{\bf K}),\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.

(C.17)

In fact, for any given point
\lambda_{0}\in[0,1]
, if
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda_{0}}(0))\neq\{0\}
,
since
[0,1]
is first countable and sequential compact,
by Step 1 in the proof of Theorem
C.4
there exists a connected open neighborhood

\mathcal{N}(\lambda_{0})
of
\lambda_{0}
in
[0,1]
such that

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda_{0}},0;{\bf K})
for any Abel group
{\bf K}

and for all
\lambda\in\mathcal{N}(\lambda_{0})
.

If
{\rm Ker}(B_{\lambda_{0}}(0))=\{0\}
, replacing
\hat{\Lambda}
by
[0,1]
in the proof of
(
C.12
) we obtain a connected open neighborhood

\mathcal{N}(\lambda_{0})
of
\lambda_{0}
in
[0,1]
such that

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})=\delta^{q}_{\mu_{\lambda_{0}}}{\bf K}

for any Abel group
{\bf K}
and all
(q,\lambda)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathcal{N}(\lambda_{0})
.

In summary, for any Abel group
{\bf K}
,
[0,1]\ni\lambda\mapsto C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})
is locally constant, and
therefore (
C.7
) holds.
In particular, this yields for any Abel group
{\bf K}

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{+}}},0;{\bf K})=C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}},0;{\bf K}),\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\},

(C.18)

Let us derive contradictions in each case.

Step 1
[
Case (e.1)
].
By (
C.16
) the third case in e.1) must occur. It contradicts (
C.18
).
For the part of “Moreover”, since
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}\in C^{2}(U^{X},\mathbb{R})

and
B_{\lambda}
is continuous as a map from
U^{X}
to
\mathscr{L}_{s}(H)

for each
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
,
as in the final paragraph in Step 1 of the proof of Theorem
C.4
we may derive from

[
32
, Corollary 2.8]
that

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf K})
for
\lambda=\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}
.
This and (
C.18
) lead to

C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{+}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})=C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})
,
which contradicts the new assumption.

Step 2
[
Case (e.2)
].
By (
C.16
),
0
is an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}
,
and
0
is a homological visible critical point of either
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
or
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}}
.
As in arguments below (
C.12
), for any Abel group
{\bf K}
we have also for
\star=+,-
,

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{\star}}},0;{\bf K})=\delta^{q}_{\mu_{\lambda^{\star}}}{\bf K}\;\;\forall q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}\;\hbox{if}\;\nu_{\lambda^{\star}}=0,

(C.19)

\displaystyle C_{q}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{\star}}},0;{\bf K})=0\;\;\forall q\notin[\mu_{\lambda^{\star}},\mu_{\lambda^{\star}}+\nu_{\lambda_{k}^{\star}}]\;\hbox{if}\;\nu_{\lambda^{\star}}>0,

(C.20)

Suppose that
0
is a homological visible critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}}
.
(The proof of another case is similar.) Then

C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0
for some Abel group
{\bf K}

and some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
, and therefore

m\in[\mu_{\lambda^{+}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}}]\cap[\mu_{\lambda^{-}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}}]

by (
C.18
) and (
C.20
). A contradiction is yielded.

Step 3
[
Case (e.3)
]. By (
C.17
),
0
is also an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}}
for each
\lambda\in\{\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}\}
. Then the third case in e.3) must occur, and so
either
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{+}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0

for some Abel group
{\bf K}
and some
m\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
or

C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})\neq 0
for some Abel group
{\bf K}^{\prime}
and some
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.
Since for any Abel group
{\bf G}
we may use

[
32
, Corollary 2.8]
to obtain
C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}|_{U^{X}},0;{\bf G})\cong C_{\ast}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda},0;{\bf G})
for
\lambda=\lambda^{+},\lambda^{-}
,
as in the proof of the case (B.2) in Step 2 of the proof of Theorem
C.4
using these and (
C.18
) we obtain
either
C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{+}}},0;{\bf K})=C_{m}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}},0;{\bf K})\neq 0
or
C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{+}}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})=C_{n}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}},0;{\bf K}^{\prime})\neq 0
.
These and (
C.19
)-(
C.20
) lead to a contradiction to the first condition in (e.3).

Step 4
[
Case (e.4)
].
Because of (
C.16
), (
C.18
) and
(
C.19
)-(
C.20
) can be used.

\bullet
If
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=0
and
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=0
, then (
C.18
) and (
C.19
) lead to

\mu_{\lambda^{+}}=\mu_{\lambda^{-}}
, and hence a contradiction.

\bullet
If
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=0
and
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}>0
, then (
C.18
) and
(
C.19
) with
\star=+
imply
C_{\mu_{\lambda^{+}}}(\mathcal{L}_{{\lambda^{-}}},0;{\bf K})={\bf K}

and therefore
\mu_{\lambda^{+}}\in[\mu_{\lambda^{-}},\mu_{\lambda^{-}}+\nu_{\lambda^{-}}]
by
(
C.20
) with
\star=-
, which is a contradiction.

\bullet
If
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}>0
and
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=0
, as in the second case we obtain

\mu_{\lambda^{-}}\in[\mu_{\lambda^{+}},\mu_{\lambda^{+}}+\nu_{\lambda^{+}}]
and so a contradiction as above.

Step 5
[
Prove the final claim
].
Let
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in[0,1]\times U
be a bifurcation point of
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(x)=0
in
[0,1]\times U
,
which is also a bifurcation point along sequences because
[0,1]
is first countable.
By contradiction suppose that
\lambda^{\ast}=\lambda^{+}
(resp.
\lambda^{\ast}=\lambda^{-}
)
in the case of
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=0
(resp.
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=0
).
The additional assumption on
A_{\lambda^{+}}
(resp.
A_{\lambda^{-}}
) implies
that
(U,X,\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{+}},A_{\lambda^{+}},B_{\lambda^{+}})

[resp.
(U,X,\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{-}},A_{\lambda^{-}},B_{\lambda^{-}})
]
also satisfies
[
52
, Hypothesis 1.1]
. Then by
[
52
, Theorem 3.1]
(see Theorem
C.6
)
we obtain
\nu_{\lambda^{+}}=\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
(resp.
\nu_{\lambda^{-}}=\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>0
), a contradiction.
∎

Because of remarks above, in most of results in
[
50
,
51
,
52
]

we either require the topology space
\Lambda
to be first countable or
change “ bifurcation point” into “bifurcation point along sequences”.
For example,
[
50
, Theorems 3.1,3.2]
should be revised as follows.

Theorem C.6
.

In
[
50
, Theorems 3.1,3.2]
, “ bifurcation point” should be changed into “bifurcation point along sequences”.

(Note that
0
is not required to be an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{\ast}}

from the proofs therein.)

Theorem C.7
.

The conclusions in
[
52
, Theorems 3.6]
should be revised as follows:

Then
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U
is a bifurcation point for the equation

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u)=0,\quad(\lambda,u)\in\Lambda\times U.

More precisely, one of the following alternatives occurs:

(i)

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not an isolated solution of the equation

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(u)=0
in
\{\lambda^{\ast}\}\times U
.

(ii)

For every
\lambda\in\Lambda
near
\lambda^{\ast}
there is a nontrivial solution
u_{\lambda}
of

A_{\lambda}(u)=0
in
U^{X}
, which converges to
0
in
X
as
\lambda\to\lambda^{\ast}
.

(iii)

For any given neighborhood
W
of
0
in
X
there is an one-sided neighborhood
\Lambda^{\ast}
of
\lambda^{\ast}
such that
for any
\lambda\in\Lambda^{\ast}\setminus\{\lambda^{\ast}\}
,
A_{\lambda}(u)=0
has at least two nontrivial solutions in
W
,
which can also be required to correspond to distinct critical values
provided that
\nu_{\lambda^{\ast}}>1
and
A_{\lambda}(u)=0
has only finitely many nontrivial solutions in
W
.

In the conclusions of
[
50
, Theorems 4.6]
, (i) is changed into the (i) above, and
the sentence “
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U^{X}
is a bifurcation point for the equation (4.15)” should be deleted.

Proof.

By the assumption f) in
[
52
, Theorems 3.6]
we obtain the first claim by the conclusion (B) in Theorem
C.4
.
Suppose that (i) is not true. Then
0\in U
is an isolated critical point of

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}
in
U
. (Of course, this implies that
0\in U^{X}
is an isolated zero of

A_{\lambda^{\ast}}(u)=0
in
U^{X}
.)
By the arguments in the first paragraph in Step 2 in the proof of Theorem
C.4
,
0\in U

is also an isolated critical point of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
for each
\lambda\in[\lambda^{\ast}-\delta,\lambda^{\ast}+\delta]
.
The other arguments in the proofs of
[
50
, Theorems 4.6]
and
[
52
, Theorems 3.6]

are valid.
∎

Theorem C.8
.

The conclusion (i) in
[
52
, Theorems 3.7]
and
[
50
, Theorems 5.12]
should be revised as follows:

(i)

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not an isolated solution of the equation

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(u)=0
in
\{\lambda^{\ast}\}\times U
.

The sentences “
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U^{X}
is a bifurcation point of (3.23)”
in
[
52
, Theorems 3.10]
and
“
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U^{X}
is a bifurcation point of (4.15)”
in
[
50
, Theorems 5.12]
are changed into
“
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U
is a bifurcation point of the equation

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(u)=0
in
\Lambda\times U
.

Theorem C.9
.

In
[
52
, Theorems 3.10]
, the words “
(\lambda^{\ast},0)\in\Lambda\times U^{X}
is a bifurcation point
for the equation (3.23); in particular” should be deleted, and the conclusion (i) should be revised as follows:

(i)

(\lambda^{\ast},0)
is not an isolated solution of the equation

D\mathcal{L}_{\lambda^{\ast}}(u)=0
in
\{\lambda^{\ast}\}\times U
.

Remark C.10
.

Using theorems in
[
49
, Appendix B]
we can also prove corresponding results with the theorems above
as supplements to
[
49
, §6]
. They and related applications will be given elsewhere.
